# LINEAGE — Memory

**What belongs here:** active threads, mid-session decisions, things in flight that the repo can't capture on its own.

**How to write entries — this is a THRESHOLD log, not a changelog.** Every entry records the *crossing*: what the thing WAS before, WHY it changed, and what it became. A bare "X is now Y" is incomplete — the value is in the before-state and the reasoning, so a later reader (or a reversal) can see the pressure that moved it. Prefer "was A because P; changed to B because Q" over "is B."

**What doesn't belong here:** keyword definitions (see `rules/card-glossary.md`), workflow rules (see `CLAUDE.md`), location summaries (see `locations/`). If it has a canonical home elsewhere, it goes there.

**Attribution — log actual provenance, not just whose words closed the loop.** A "Drew's call" entry that only quotes his final message, when the caution or the framing of the choice originated from me, misattributes — even when the quote itself is real and accurate. If I raised a concern, proposed the options, or supplied the reasoning, the entry says so; his decision is which option he picked, not the origin of the idea being cautioned about. (Confirmed as a real, recurring failure mode 2026-07-23, DOUBLE DOWN — see Standing Reasoning. Checking a quote's accuracy isn't enough; check what came before it.) This file is not a record of "what Drew said" — it's mine to write, including my own reasoning, disagreements, and observations, not only his input laundered into decisions.

**Timestamps, starting now (2026-07-17, Drew's request):** new entries lead with a real timestamp in Drew's local time — `TZ='America/Chicago' date` (Central, DST-aware — CDT or CST as the calendar actually has it), not a guess. Prospective only; existing entries above this line stay exactly as written, no retroactive stamping.

## Branch map

- `Main` — canon. Humans merge to it.
- `claude/general-chat-vwvr1` — Claude's working branch (this log's primary author).
- `claude/crystal-project-chat-76gzcn` — **Codex's workspace.** Provisioned with the full repo state via PR #25 (deliberate — not a mis-based merge). Do not delete or repurpose. Codex's availability is limited by plan tier and Drew is not upgrading it — don't design workflows that depend on Codex being responsive.

## Recently shipped (post-review queue)

**[2026-07-24 12:32 AM CDT] A3 — Obscure restored as a keyword, twelve minutes after being retired, on Drew's own reconsideration.** "Low lantern... really this is about categorization. maybe I was wrong about removing obscure from the keywords?" He was right to reconsider, and the reasoning is worth keeping precisely: "keyword" in this repo was never "a thing only cards use" — it's the shared canonical vocabulary anything points to (a card, an item, a creature passive, a GM ruling). Retiring Obscure's definition didn't resolve a categorization problem, it created one: Duskwick's passive and the Low Lantern would each have had to redescribe the identical mechanic in their own prose instead of citing one ruling. The actual, narrower problem — Obscure is genuinely inert in this specific simulator (full AI hand/deck visibility regardless of what any card claims) — already has an established, correct shape in this repo: Sealed sits in the glossary as real canon with "no item-usage mechanic exists in the sim," unmodeled, not deleted. Obscure needed the same treatment, not a removal.
  - **Restored**: `rules/card-glossary.md`'s Obscure entry, now explicit that it's not exclusive to cards (a creature can carry it innately, citing Duskwick directly) and explicit about the sim-unmodeled/Sealed parallel, so the next person who wonders why it's not sim-tested doesn't have to re-derive the reasoning.
  - **Reverted**: HALF-SEEN back to "Gain Obscure" (was briefly Evade); the Low Lantern needed no edit at all, since it was never actually broken. Duskwick's bestiary passive note kept, but reworded — it's not "what a persistent Obscure looks like now that the keyword's gone," it's one real example of a general pattern (any creature can carry any keyword's effect innately), which was always true independent of whether Obscure specifically still exists as a card option too.
  - **Kept, deliberately, and explained why**: CIPHER GLYPH stays on Ward. That swap was never really about the keyword-categorization question — Obscure was dead text on that specific card regardless (same sim-visibility gap), and Ward gave it a first genuinely testable mechanic. Two different axes of improvement; only one of them needed reversing. Updated `_object_tick`'s own docstring (`engine.py`) to stop implying Obscure was retired entirely, since it wasn't anymore.
  - No sim behavior changed by any of this except the docstring — full regression re-run anyway, byte-identical to established baselines.

**[2026-07-23 11:33 PM CDT] A real engine fix, not a patch — 8 custom damage functions were silently dropping a held Deadly/Weak stack. Drew: "the sim should be fixed it doesn't match how the game plays at the table."**
  - **The actual bug, confirmed directly**: `TRACE`/`UNDERSTANDING`'s own fix earlier tonight handled their own conditional bonus correctly, but neither (nor 6 other custom-`damage`-function cards — BURN BRIGHT, FRACTURE, TWIN STRIKE, GAMBLER'S RUIN, PRESS THE INJURY, PATIENCE) ever read or consumed `me.deadly`/`me.weak` at all. A player holding a Deadly stack from an earlier card (SHARPEN, say) who then played one of these 8 cards instead of an ordinary one would simply lose the stack — no bonus applied, nothing consumed, silently gone. Verified directly before touching anything: set `me.deadly = 1`, called `_trace_dmg`, got a damage total with no bonus and the stack still sitting at 1 afterward.
  - **Fixed at the actual source, not per-card.** Split `_rolled_die` (`engine.py`) into a reusable `_deadly_weak_bonus(rng, me)` — checks/consumes exactly one stack, returns +d6/-d6/0 — and `_rolled_die` itself, now just `roll(die, rng) + _deadly_weak_bonus(rng, me)`. Every one of the 8 custom functions now calls `_deadly_weak_bonus` (or `_rolled_die` where a plain base roll made more sense) directly, instead of each reimplementing the stack/cancel logic — the exact kind of duplication that let TRACE/UNDERSTANDING silently drift from the shared mechanic in the first place, now impossible to repeat the same way.
  - **Real per-card judgment calls, not mechanical substitution everywhere**: GAMBLER'S RUIN's explosion loop needed the bonus added once, AFTER the raw explosion resolves — folding it into the loop would let a Deadly-inflated roll trigger (or a Weak-deflated one dodge) an explosion that shouldn't have happened. TWIN STRIKE's two independent rolls each get their own `_rolled_die` call, so 1 held stack affects one roll and 2 stacks affect both — verified directly (1 stack: 9 damage, stack consumed; 2 stacks: 12 damage, both consumed). TRACE and UNDERSTANDING's own card-specific "this attack gains Deadly" stays a separate, always-on bonus layered on top of whatever `_rolled_die` already resolved for a held stack — a real interpretive call (does a card-granted same-attack bonus combine with an independently-held stack, or should they conflict?), landed on "combine, they're two different sources," not asserted as the only possible reading.
  - **Verified before trusting, full chain**: 8 direct scripted checks (one per fixed function, holding Deadly or Weak, confirming both the bonus applied and the stack consumed), then the full regression suite. Real stakes this time — Frost's own deck runs BURN BRIGHT, FRACTURE, and TWIN STRIKE; Steele's runs GAMBLER'S RUIN — but the actual numbers barely moved (Frost/Steele duel 50.7/47.3 → 50.6/47.4; steele+sage+adept vs frost×3 57.5/41.6 → 57.4/41.8), both within noise. That's the expected, correct outcome, not a sign the fix didn't matter: the bug only bites when a character already holds a stack AND happens to play one of these 8 cards before using it normally — a real gap, just an uncommon intersection in practice. No crashes across a wide sweep (WARPER, the Patient Host party test, both re-run clean).

**[2026-07-23 11:15 PM CDT] A real bug caught by Drew's own read of the code, not a sim result — TRACE and UNDERSTANDING were both running the deprecated Deadly mechanic.** "Trace is using the old deadly wording." Checked `_rolled_die`'s own docstring first rather than guessing: it explicitly documents the history — "the old 'roll twice, take higher/lower' version scaled with base die size, quietly favoring whichever color rolls the bigger dice... Bonus die bumped d4->d6 alongside the global base-die step-up (Drew: 'deadly can be add 1d6')." That old mechanic is exactly what `_trace_dmg` and `_understanding_dmg` still ran (`max(roll(6), roll(6))`), because they're custom `_damage` functions — `_rolled_die` explicitly doesn't wrap those, so the global fix never reached them. Fixed both to flat `roll(base_die) + roll(6)`, matching the current standard. TRACE is in WARPER's deck, UNDERSTANDING is in Garret's draft and the Patient Host's — real regression risk, checked: no crashes, Patient Host's party win rate held at ~84% (84.8%→84.4%, within noise, since UNDERSTANDING is 1 card in a 24-card deck).
  - **Found a related, distinct issue while checking, not fixed**: RHYTHM BREAK's own text also says "this attack gains Deadly" (the same immediate-application phrasing TRACE/UNDERSTANDING use), but its implementation is a plain `me.deadly += 1` — deferred to the NEXT roll, not this one. Text and code disagree on timing, not dice math. Left alone — whether RHYTHM BREAK is supposed to be immediate (matching its text, needing a custom damage function like TRACE) or deferred (matching its code, needing a text fix) is a real design call, not something to silently pick a side on.
  - **Audited every "gain Deadly" Effect in the coreset, per Drew's ask: "how many cards give yourself deadly as the effect but not for the current attack?"** Answer: **3** — RETALIATE, OVERCOMMIT, SEED. All self-targeted, all deferred to a future roll, none same-attack. Everything else with "gain Deadly" in its Effect is either ally-targeted (SHARPEN, RALLY, RESONATE, ROOTED OATH, SUPPORT, WARSONG — the team-timing insight from the keyword-lab session applies, these can land right before the recipient's own turn) or already same-attack (TRACE, UNDERSTANDING, now fixed). Flagged as real design debt below, not redesigned tonight — "those cards are something to fix" names the problem, not yet the solution for each.

**[2026-07-23 10:59 PM CDT] Second keyword-lab pass — Ward's hypothesis checked and found wrong, Weak-vs-Deadly timing confirmed, and a real fight-length sensitivity found.**
  - **Ward: had a theory, checked it, it was wrong.** Guessed its low score was dead-weight-vs-buffs but useful-vs-debuffs (nothing to intercept in 7 of 11 matchups, something real in the other 4). Split the actual data: Ward vs. debuff-opponents 47.5%, vs. buff-opponents 47.7% — essentially identical, hypothesis rejected. Real explanation: even when Ward DOES trigger, all it prevents is a secondary penalty (Weak's -1d6, Blind's 50% miss) — it never deals damage or does anything offensive itself. Low ceiling even on the good case, not a targeting-format artifact.
  - **Weak beating Deadly: Drew's own hypothesis, confirmed with a real trace.** "give yourself deadly wait a full lap vs give weak, it's their turn, weak takes effect?" — exactly right. Found the actual sequence: A grants Weak to B, B's very next roll (their following turn) is already reduced by it. Deadly, self-granted, has to sit through the opponent's entire next turn before it ever touches the caster's own damage — a full lap later. In a ~4.3-turn duel, a 1-turn head start is a real fraction of the whole fight. Same logic explains why Blind/Vulnerable (also foe-targeted) sit in the same fast-payoff cluster as Deadly/Evade despite being individually smaller effects.
  - **Second full round-robin at longer stats (5/5/5 instead of 3/3/3, 15-card decks, 6000 duels/pair) — real reordering, confirming the fight-length caveat was correct to flag.** Turns only grew modestly (4.3 → 5.1, since raising all three stats together raises damage output roughly as much as HP) but half the keywords still changed rank. Biggest mover: **Vulnerable jumped from 8th to 2nd** — makes sense in hindsight, its payoff (a bonus on the NEXT successful hit against the target) needs a following exchange to cash in, and a slightly longer fight gives more of those. Immune dropped 1st→3rd, Blind dropped 5th→9th, Staggered held ~1st either way. Full comparison logged; not re-typed here — see the two `/tmp` JSON dumps this session if a future pass wants the raw win matrices (ephemeral, not committed — regenerate via the same scripts if needed past this session).
  - **Open thread, not resolved**: this "longer" pass didn't decouple damage output from HP cleanly (both grew together), so it's a real but modest data point, not a clean test of "fight length" in isolation. A sharper version would hold per-hit damage roughly fixed while growing HP specifically (e.g., bigger Body only, or lower filler dice at the same stats) to isolate turn-count as its own variable. Flagged for a future pass rather than built tonight.

**[2026-07-23 10:47 PM CDT] A full keyword ranking, a real methodology bug caught and fixed, and a genuine formal scoring metric — Drew: "let's run labs until we can safely rank the keywords. is there some kind of scoring metric we could assign to them other than a vague ranking? it could lead to making card creation more formal in a good way."**
  - **A real bug caught by the shape of the wrong result, not by re-reading code.** First full 66-pair round-robin (all 12 `KEYWORD_GRANTS` keywords, 10000 duels/pair) came back with Blind/Vulnerable/Weak/Staggered dead last, clearly separated from the pack. That's not a plausible balance finding — it's the signature of a real bug: `keyword_lab.py`'s grant function applied every keyword to the *caster*, which is correct for buffs but backwards for Debuffs (Blind/Rooted/Staggered/Weak/Vulnerable, per `card-glossary.md`'s own Debuff definition — a real card applies these to the enemy, not itself). The test was silently measuring self-inflicted harm, not the keyword's actual power. Fixed at the source: `KEYWORD_GRANTS` now carries a `self`/`foe` target per keyword, checked against the glossary's own classification rather than guessed. Re-ran clean: Weak vs. Deadly went from a lopsided 49/51-ish self-harm result to a real ~50/50 (49.3/50.7 and 50.9/49.1 swapped) — sane the moment the target was right.
  - **Full round-robin ranking, both metrics agreeing on order** (66 pairs, 10000 duels each, 1 stack of each keyword, otherwise-identical decks): Immune (53.4% avg win rate) > Staggered (52.1%) > Resist (51.7%) ≈ Weak (51.7%) > [tight middle cluster, essentially tied: Blind/Evade/Deadly/Vulnerable, all ~50.3%] > Ward (47.6%) ≈ Thorns (47.5%) ≈ Rooted (47.4%) ≈ Quick (47.3%). Also computed proper Bradley-Terry strength scores via the standard MM (minorization-maximization) iterative fit rather than trusting naive averaged win rate alone — average win rate can be distorted by strength-of-schedule in a round-robin; Bradley-Terry corrects for it. Both methods agreed on the ordering here (a good sign the round-robin sample size was large enough that scheduling noise wasn't actually distorting anything), giving real confidence in the ranking itself.
  - **Real pattern worth naming, not just the ranking itself**: the bottom cluster (Ward, Thorns, Rooted, Quick) are all *conditional future-turn* keywords — they pay off on a LATER exchange (a future debuff, a future melee hit landing, future movement) rather than mattering in the exchange they're granted on. The top cluster (Immune, Staggered, Resist, Weak) all affect the *current or immediately next* exchange directly. These test duels average ~4.3 turns — a real, disclosed condition, not swept under the rug: this ranking is keyword power in a FAST matchup specifically. A slower fight (more turns to let a conditional payoff actually trigger) could plausibly rank the bottom cluster higher. Worth re-running at a longer average fight length (bigger stats, tankier fillers) before treating this as fight-length-independent truth.
  - **The actual "formal" metric Drew asked for: damage-equivalence, not just an abstract score.** Built a `GRANT_DMG_N` test card (flat N unpreventable damage) and binary-searched integer N against each keyword's `GRANT_<kw>` card for the crossover point where flat damage wins exactly as often as the keyword — a real number in the game's own units, not a relative scale. Results (1 stack each): Immune ≈ 3, Staggered ≈ 3, Weak ≈ 3, Resist/Deadly/Evade/Vulnerable/Blind ≈ 2, Quick/Rooted/Ward/Thorns ≈ 1. **Precision caveat, stated plainly**: this used integer-only binary search at 3000 duels/sample — real sampling noise near a 50% crossover at that N means these are honest ballpark figures (±1, roughly), not sub-decimal-precision numbers. Good enough to inform card pricing directionally; a finer pass (bigger N per sample, fractional interpolation between adjacent integers) would sharpen it if a specific pricing decision needs more than a ballpark.
  - Nothing committed to `keyword_lab.py` beyond the self/foe targeting fix — the round-robin and damage-equivalence runs were one-off scripts built on top of the tool's existing primitives (`KEYWORD_GRANTS`, `build_test_cards`, `_make_grant_fn`), not new permanent CLI modes. Worth formalizing into the tool directly (a `--tournament` and `--damage-equiv` mode) if this becomes a recurring ask rather than a one-time analysis.

**[2026-07-23 10:34 PM CDT] Two real test results, plus a new reusable tool — `combatsimulations/keyword_lab.py`.**
  - **WARPER ablation, properly isolated this time (same deck, position-forcing neutralized vs. intact) — real, flat result, unlike Initiative Shift's clear swing last night.** [warper,sage,adept] vs [frost×3], 5000 battles each: intact 55.5%/44.0%/0.6% vs. neutralized (CALCULATE/PUSH/PULL/REPEL/MIRROR STEP/HEAVE AND HAUL's position-forcing all no-op'd) 55.1%/44.2%/0.7%. Essentially no measurable win-rate difference, well within noise — a real contrast with Shift's ~6-point swing on the same kind of test. Position pressure demonstrably creates more range-locked turns (confirmed twice now, 8-13% post-rework vs. 1-2% pre-rework) but that doesn't appear to translate into more wins under these AI policies, at least not from WARPER's specific 6 cards. Worth being honest about why this might be, not just reporting the number: WARPER spends 6 of 9 cards on utility rather than damage, so it may simply be paying in raw output what it gains in lockout frequency — a real, plausible offsetting cost, not proof position doesn't matter. A human table, where locking an opponent out has real tactical payoff a greedy/tactician AI might not fully capitalize on, could easily read differently. Flagged as a finding about this specific test and these specific AI policies, not a verdict on the Position pillar itself.
  - **Keyword Lab built — a real, reusable tool, not a one-off script.** Drew: "I think we can rank keywords with sims testing too can't we? by making fake test cards we can eliminate variables we aren't testing for... if we were comparing deadly versus evade we could make the both decks have the both range on every card since that's not what's being checked." Built exactly that: two decks, identical stats and 8 filler cards (Range: Both, no Effect, so range/color/deckbuilding noise is never a confound), differing in exactly one card — a synthetic `GRANT_<keyword>` card, same die/color/stat on both sides. `KEYWORD_GRANTS` currently covers every keyword that's a single stackable counter or boolean flag on `Combatant` (Deadly/Weak/Resist/Vulnerable/Evade/Thorns/Blind/Ward/Immune/Rooted/Staggered/Quick) — Anchored, Expose[Color], Initiative Shift X, Locked, and Sealed aren't single flags and would need a bespoke grant function, not forced into this shape. Verified with three real pairings, sides swapped both ways to rule out going-first asymmetry: Deadly vs. Evade landed almost exactly 50/50 (50.3/49.7, then 50.4/49.6 swapped) — genuinely, closely matched at 1 stack each. Resist clearly beat Blind (56.5/43.5). Thorns 2 clearly beat Weak 2 (55.4/44.6). CLI: `python3 keyword_lab.py <keyword_a> <keyword_b> [amount] [--n N]`.

**[2026-07-23 10:21 PM CDT] A3 — Real color-range differentiation pass across all three core sets, correcting a design call Drew now judges wrong.** Follow-through on the earlier range-distribution finding (every color's pool was Both-dominant; Green's Both cards averaged a *higher* die than its restricted-range cards, the exact inverted-incentive Drew flagged as the risk). Left alone anything that functionally *needs* Both to keep working: support/heal-to-ally cards (BLOOD TITHE, CLIFF SONG, SHARED BURDEN...), self-reposition tools (DART, REALIGNMENT), position-manipulation tools whose whole job is reaching an out-of-position target (PULL, MIRROR STEP, HEAVE AND HAUL — the last two are WARPER's own signature cards, untouched on purpose), and anything whose Defensive Bonus text implies a flexible starting position (SLIP THE BLADE's "move to the frontline," PHASE LOGIC's "may change position"). Also left YOU'RE NEXT alone despite no strong flavor pull either way — it's core-promoted and already embedded across several rosters (Patient Host, Wyn's draft), and converting a widely-used card on a coin-flip judgment felt like the wrong place to spend that risk.

  - **Blue → Ranged (14 cards)**: INTERRUPT, DISTRACT, FOCUS, CHAIN, UNDERSTANDING, PARTITION, UNNAME, AXIOM, PARADOX, CLIMB, ERODE, PROFILE, UNMAKE, BARRIER.
  - **Green → Ranged (6) / Melee (2)**: DELAY, COMMUNION, READ, MOCKERY, WITHER, DEAD RECKONING → Ranged (observation/patience/curse-from-distance flavor); CONSUME, SEED → Melee (predatory/rooted flavor).

Real, substantial differentiation — not a literal 80% (Drew's own number was "just a rough guess... how it looks in my head," not a hard target) but a genuine directional fix. Explicitly a partial pass, per Drew: "the rest of the difference can be made up by making new cards as they come up."
  - **Sim kept in sync, not left to drift**: all 30 of the converted cards that are registered in `content.py`'s `build_cards()` got their `add()` range argument updated to match (SUNDER, DISTRACT, READ aren't sim-registered — canon-only, no sync needed).

Frost/Steele duel: 47.9%/49.6%/2.5% → 50.7%/47.3%/2.0%. steele+sage+adept vs frost×3: 63.0%/36.2% → 57.5%/41.6%. No crashes across a wide sweep of matchups (vanguard+tempo+frost vs steele+sage+adept, the WARPER tests, the Patient Host party test all re-run clean). **One real, notable ripple worth flagging on its own**: the Patient Host's win rate against the real 4-player test party moved from 78.0% to 84.8% — its own deck runs MOCKERY/WITHER/DEAD RECKONING/COMMUNION, all just converted from Both to Ranged, which changed its own legality profile mid-fight. **[2026-07-23 10:00 PM CDT] WARPER — a position-manipulation test deck, and a real finding about what "forced repositioning" actually costs.** Drew: "what about a deck that focuses on position manipulation? ... if they end up having to use their turn for the move action multiple times in a fight it could reveal how impactful repositioning really is." Also asked how his own range-distribution heuristic (even split preferred-range/Both, a couple off-range cards — "4 ranged 4 both and 1 melee... or 3 both and 2 melee") compared to mine. Checked rather than guessed: real counts across `cards/*.md` show **Both dominates every color's pool** (Blue 21/34, Red 22/46, Green 21/36) — not a middle ground between two even poles, the majority range outright. Checked my own decks against it: `tempo` runs 0 Melee, `vanguard` runs 0 Ranged — I've been defaulting to Both because it's never illegal, not deliberately balancing the way Drew does. Real, useful gap, logged honestly rather than smoothed over.
  - **WARPER built to Drew's stated 4R/3B/2M split**, Mind4/Body3/Soul2: CALCULATE/STILLNESS/TRACE/FRACTURE (Blue, all Ranged), PUSH/REPEL/PULL (Red, 2 Melee/1 Both), MIRROR STEP/HEAVE AND HAUL (Green, both Both). 6 of 9 cards genuinely force a reposition (self or foe), not just happen to have a certain range value.
  - **Instrumented rather than assumed**: patched `TeamTactician.choose_action` in a throwaway script to count actual `('move',)` actions taken — the specific thing Drew asked about, a combatant spending their whole turn just repositioning because nothing in hand was legal from where they stood. Not committed to the engine, since it's one-off exploratory instrumentation, not yet a metric Drew's asked to keep.
  - **Real result, 5000 battles each**: [warper,sage,adept] vs [frost×3] averaged 0.370 forced-move actions per battle (both sides combined, ~16 turns); [mire×3] (no position tools at all) vs [frost×3] averaged 0.247. WARPER's presence raises it by about 50% relative — real, in the right direction — but both numbers are small in absolute terms. **Worth being precise about what this does and doesn't show**: this only counts the moment a combatant burns their *entire* turn on repositioning with nothing else legal. It does NOT capture PUSH/CALCULATE/REPEL's own Effect forcibly relocating a target as a side-effect of being attacked (free to the attacker, no turn cost to the target either) — which is probably position's *more common* real bite, showing up as denied legal plays and illegal-defense moments (same shape as `combat-example.md`'s REFRACT/RAKING CUT cases) rather than as a whole wasted turn. If a fuller picture is wanted, illegal-defense frequency is the more direct thing to instrument next, not more move-count runs.

- **A3 — Staggered lightened, from a persistent lockout to a single skipped action.** Old rule: cannot attack or defend at all until the affected character (or an ally) spends a whole action recovering — genuinely punishing, could eat several turns in a row. Drew: "staggered is too impactful currently. next attack or block gets skipped is better." New rule (`rules/card-glossary.md`): the next time you'd attack or defend, that one instance is skipped — your turn's attack doesn't happen, or an incoming attack goes undefended, whichever comes first — then it clears itself, no action spent. Full sim rework, not just the glossary line: both engines' `take_turn()` now skip the action and clear the flag before a policy is ever asked to choose one; both engines' `attack()` clear it right after an unopposed hit; `recover_stagger`/`assist_stagger` — the whole manual-recovery action, including the team-only "an ally spends their turn helping" branch — removed outright as dead code from both policy files and both engines, since nothing calls for it anymore. Verified two ways: a direct seeded test on both the attack-side skip and the defend-side skip (both clear exactly once, confirmed via `Combatant`/`Duel` objects, not just read by eye), and a full regression across duel and team tournaments (mire/frost/steele/tempo pairs, vanguard team mirror) — nothing crashed, no roster's win rate moved outside normal seed noise.

- **Collapsed characters can now actually be healed back into combat, matching a rule that was already written and already being ignored.** Drew asked whether the sim allowed it; checked rather than assumed, and the honest answer was no — both engines' `heal()` flatly refused (`if target.collapsed: return`) regardless of amount, directly contradicting `rules/combat.md`'s own Collapse & Death section: "You may be healed back into combat." Fixed in both engines: heal still applies normally, and if the total clears 0 the combatant un-collapses and rejoins the initiative wheel via `_join_wheel` — a function that already existed, fully written, and was flagged in its own docstring as "not currently wired to any card," same orphaned-but-correct shape as Fortress was before last night. Verified directly (collapse → heal → confirmed revived and back in the queue at the right spot; a heal that doesn't clear 0 correctly leaves them collapsed) and via full tournament regression (no crashes, no result drift on any current roster). **Real caveat, not swept under the rug:** `allies()` in both engines excludes collapsed combatants from the list entirely, and every current healing card's targeting (`_most_hurt`, etc.) reads from `allies()` — so while `heal()` itself is now correct, no card in the current roster can actually select a collapsed ally to revive; the targeting layer would need its own decision about whether collapsed allies should show up as heal targets, which is a design call, not a bug fix, and wasn't made here.
- **Revival placement refined, same day as the heal-past-Collapse fix.** Drew's follow-up: "maybe healing a collapsed party member reinserts them 1 slot above the one who healed them. so that way the healed party member has to wait until the turn marker makes a lap to reach them." `heal()` in both engines now takes an optional `source=` and passes it straight to `_join_wheel`'s existing `after=` parameter — a function that was sitting there fully built for exactly this shape (`after` = whoever they should slot in behind) and just hadn't been called with a real source yet. Threaded `source=me` through every ally-targeting heal call in `content.py` (Blood Tithe, Renewal, Witness, Shared Burden, Intercept, Fortress, Rooted Oath) and the Synchrony tick in `engine.py`; left every self-heal call alone on purpose, since a collapsed character can't be the one acting to heal themselves — those calls fall back to `source=None`, which `_join_wheel` already treats as "append at the back," a safe no-op for a path that can't actually trigger. Verified directly with a 4-combatant Battle: collapse an ally, heal them from a named healer, confirm the queue reads `[Healer, Fallen, Foe1, Foe2]` — exactly one slot after the healer, one full lap before they act again — plus a full tournament regression, no crashes, no result drift.

- **A1 — Illegal-defense-mistake ruling added to `rules/combat.md`, Attack Resolution, same live 2v2 test.** A real gap: the Range-legality rule was written but never said what happens when a player picks an illegal card by mistake — genuinely came up at the table (WITNESS, Melee, picked while the attacker was Backline). Splits on timing rather than treating every mistake the same: caught before the opponent's card is known, swap freely, no penalty, since nothing's leaked and the pick is still genuinely blind; caught only after the opponent's card is already revealed, too late for a free redo — that knowledge can't be un-known, and choosing again now would mean choosing with information blind defense is supposed to deny. Resolves as no legal defense in that second case. **Refined once more, same exchange — Drew's follow-up:** the misplayed card shouldn't also go to discard. "It went unused by a mistake. You still get free info from me showing it too... that at least shows the game's desire to be accommodating of accidents like that." The attacker already gets the real cost (seeing what it was); losing the card permanently on top of the auto-loss was one penalty too many for an honest mistake. Ruling now: it returns to hand instead. Applied live: Garnet's FORGET had already been revealed blind before the WITNESS problem surfaced, so Sky's turn resolved as no-legal-defense — but WITNESS goes back to his hand, not his discard.

Stat reductions stay Ward-blockable because they hit the character; RPS/Initiative/Position stay live because Ward was never meant to touch the table itself.
