- **A3 — Wait can no longer be used two turns in a row, closing a genuine soft-lock the live 2v2 test never happened to trigger.** Drew, the morning after: "either one of our sides in that test match could have soft locked the game under rules as written... if crimson and sky keep waiting to act right after each other forever, you coulda done the same." Checked and confirmed real before agreeing to anything: nothing in the old Wait rule capped how often it could be reused, so two combatants trading Waits back and forth could deny each other forever with no rule ever forcing a real action. Fixed with a new, distinct marker (explicitly not Staggered's skip marker, just borrowing the same self-clearing shape): Waiting marks your token, the very next turn Wait is unavailable and you must take a real action, then the mark clears. Caps the worst case at one mutual dodge per side, not an infinite chain. Landed on the final shape after two real refinements in the same conversation, both worth recording since they show the actual design process, not just the outcome: (1) Wait's reposition simplified from "choose X, shift by X" to "pick your slot directly, still later-only" — confirmed explicitly this doesn't relax the wheel's real geometric constraint (you're standing at the marker, "earlier" doesn't exist this lap), just a cleaner player-facing interface for the same math; (2) Drew's original proposal also folded a Frontline/Backline flip into Wait itself, but a genuine two-way miscommunication (my clarifying question about Move/Wait timing got read as advocacy for keeping them separate) surfaced a real fork — flagged it explicitly rather than silently building on the misread, gave Drew both options honestly, and he chose to keep Wait and Move fully separate on the merits once it was spelled out plainly, not because of how the question happened to be phrased. Applied to `rules/combat.md`'s actions table and Waiting section. No sim change needed — Wait/Pass has been intentionally unmodeled in the simulator all along. **Confirmed the next morning, worth keeping since it answers a question a future reader would ask:** splitting Wait and Move didn't actually cost the combined flexibility Drew's original bundled proposal wanted. The new marker only blocks *Wait* specifically, not Move — so Wait this turn (land wherever), then Move is simply sitting there as a fully legal choice the turn after, since it was never restricted in the first place. Same two adjustments as the original one-action idea, just paced across two turns instead of bundled into one — "you really used up your action this lap but you got both adjustments. That sounds fair." Drew, on the source: borrowed the closing-a-mutual-stall shape from CT/delay-style turn engines (FFX and its descendants) rather than inventing the diagnosis from scratch — real precedent, not just convenient.
- **A3 — Three new Initiative Shift payoff cards (RETALIATE, WARSONG, REBUTTAL), plus a real redefinition of Deadly/Weak they exposed, plus a genuine pre-existing engine bug the new cards were the first thing to actually trigger.** Started from Drew's own diagnosis of last night's test: Initiative Shift got skipped in deckbuilding even though (corrected mid-conversation, see below) a 4-seat fight is documented as the format's *peak* impact for the mechanic, not a weak one — so the fix is cards that make shifting pay off directly, not just hope decks pick it up. Full arc, since the process is as worth keeping as the result:
  - **Trigger timing, precisely nailed down after one real misfire.** First draft used "since your last turn" (a broad window); Drew corrected it to something much sharper: check specifically whoever's token resolved *immediately before* this one, positionally — not a time window at all. "This incentivizes using Wait and Initiative Shift for timing" — the payoff and the wheel-positioning game are meant to be the same decision. Confirmed with his own worked example (ally hits, your turn is next, condition met) before writing any card text.
  - **Three cards, one shape, three relationships to the trigger:** RETALIATE (Red) needs an enemy to have hit *you specifically* — the narrowest condition, Red's "commitment" identity; WARSONG (Green) needs any *ally* to have connected — the loosest condition, paying out to the whole team; REBUTTAL (Blue) needs any *enemy* to have connected, punishing that same enemy with Staggered — pure control, doesn't need to have landed on you. `cards/red-body.md`, `cards/green-soul.md`, `cards/blue-mind.md`.
  - **Red's payoff scaled up hard once the real restriction was named aloud** — enemy has to go right before you, has to hit you specifically, and you still need a legal, undefeated attack back at that same enemy. Drew: "if we passed on it in the format where it has the most impact then the cards might be underpowered" logic, applied here directly: "if it's that restrictive then the payoff should be larger. much larger." Landed on Deadly ×2 over a flat damage number, keeping the card doing one thing.
  - **A real balance conversation, not a rubber stamp, on all three:** conceded my own CC-lockout worry on Rebuttal was overstated once Drew pointed at actual data (Crimson's deck cycled about 1.5 times over 18 turns last night) and the RPS base-rate (~1/3 clean-win chance) — a "loop" that's resource- and probability-gated isn't a hard lock, and I was wrong to treat it like one. Green's "all allies" scaling concern held up, but narrowed to what it actually is: an *enemy-encounter-design* caution (player parties cap at 4, so the player-facing card is bounded; a GM handing multiple enemies the same shape in an uncapped group is the real risk, not the card itself). Red's Melee range dropped to Both once the trigger's own restriction was judged sufficient without compounding a range gate on top.
  - **Deadly/Weak redefined system-wide, from "roll twice, take higher/lower" to a flat ±1d4** — Drew's own catch, checked against real math before agreeing: `E[max of 2 rolls]` scales with die size (`(n+1)(4n-1)/(6n)`), so the old mechanic was worth +0.25 on a d2 and +1.65 on a d10 — a 6.6× spread. Red owns the big dice (d8 Strike, d10 Sacrifice Strike); Green mostly rolls d2s post-review. Every card granting Deadly — including two of these three new ones — was quietly paying its best value to whichever color held the bigger die, not to whoever the effect was built for. Flat +1d4 (Weak: flat −1d4, confirmed symmetric on the spot) fixes it completely: identical expected value no matter the base die. `rules/card-glossary.md` and `combatsimulations/engine.py`'s `_rolled_die` — wide blast radius (RALLY, ROOTED OATH, SHARPEN, ALIGN, STUDY, DEAD RECKONING, TWIN STRIKE, and the three new cards all reference Deadly/Weak by name), but zero card *text* needed touching — the mechanic is defined centrally and every reference inherits the new behavior automatically.
  - **Real pre-existing engine bug found building the new cards, not caused by them:** WARSONG's Defensive Bonus (Initiative Shift +2 to an ally) crashed team battles the instant a fight thinned to exactly 2 remaining combatants — `_apply_shift`'s path-walking logic assumed a shift that "crosses the marker" always physically walks through slot 0, but a shift whose magnitude is an exact multiple of the current seat count (here, +2 against a 2-seat wheel) lands the target back on its *own* starting slot with zero actual walk — `path.index(0)` then fails because 0 was never visited. Pre-existing bug, unrelated to anything new about these cards except that WARSONG was the first card ever to apply a shift matching this exact arithmetic in live testing. Fixed by special-casing `landing == i`: no one else is actually displaced (the target's own lap doesn't touch anyone else's relative order), but the marker-crossing consequence — bonus turn for a positive shift, shift-skip for a negative one — still lands on the target itself, since they did cross the marker during the lap even though they end up back where they started. Verified directly (queue provably unchanged, bonus turn provably granted) and via a 10,000-battle stress test on a deck built specifically to keep all three new cards live in every hand — zero crashes, after one real fix.
  - Names: RETALIATE, WARSONG, REBUTTAL — the last one chosen over "Counter-frame" (reads mechanical) and "Cross-apply" (clever but obscure rhetoric jargon) for landing clean and immediate, matching the register of Axiom/Paradox/Refract.
  - Tested and shipped together: scripted verification of the Deadly/Weak math (statistical, matches predicted averages to within noise) and all three cards' trigger logic (positive and negative controls) directly against real `Duel`/`Battle` objects, plus the full existing duel/team regression suite (frost/steele/mire/volk/tempo/warden/sage/adept, vanguard/mire team battles, steele+sage+adept vs frost×3, Patient Host 1v3) — no drift outside normal seed noise anywhere. Print sheets regenerated.
  - **Two real follow-up corrections from Drew, same review, both caught before I'd have found them myself:** (1) WARSONG's Effect used `_team(engine, me)` (self + allies), which silently included the caster — Drew: "warsong only gives deadly to allies not yourself." Checked URGENCY's own existing "all allies" implementation first rather than guessing, and it already excludes self (`engine.allies(me)` directly) — my deviation was an oversight, not a deliberate choice, now matching established convention. Confirmed as a real question, not just a fix: in a 1v1 Duel this makes WARSONG's Effect a complete no-op (no allies exist to buff) — verified directly, and accepted as correct, same shape as every other team-play Green card (Resonate, Support, etc.) being inert in a duel. (2) The 3-seat dampening rule (`_apply_shift`, "a shift of ±1 becomes a no-op with 3 or fewer combatants") was gated on `len(queue) == 3` — exact equality, never `<=`. Drew: "when the number of active combatants is 3 or less the impact of initiative shifts gets reduced by 1." Fixed to `<= 3`. **Bigger consequence than the one-line fix suggests, flagged explicitly rather than buried:** a Duel's queue is always exactly 2 combatants for the entire fight, so under the old `==3` gate, dampening had *never once* applied inside any 1v1 duel this entire session, regardless of intent — INTERRUPT, MOCKERY, DELAY, URGENCY have all been running undamped in every duel-format regression run so far. The corrected `<=3` turns dampening on retroactively for every duel, not just the WARSONG-at-2-seats case that prompted the question. Full regression re-run after both fixes (frost/steele, tempo/warden, vanguard/mire team battles, Patient Host 1v3) — clean, no crashes — but the historical duel balance numbers logged earlier this project for Initiative-Shift-heavy matchups (the Blue/Red/Green archetype analysis, tempo's own numbers) were measured *before* this dampening ever actually applied in duels. Surfaced to Drew directly rather than silently absorbed; whether the wider retroactive change is desired as-is or wants a duel-specific carve-out is his call, not decided here. **Confirmed the same session:** "yeah of course let's have this impact reduction in duels. clean ≤ 3." Standing as shipped, no carve-out. Drew's own read afterward: "what an excellent system probe" — one card built to close a deckbuilding gap surfaced a crash, a self-targeting bug, and a scope error that had been silently under-applying for the entire session.
- **A2 ⚑ nine new build archetypes named, two cards built as their first real seeds.** Drew, stepping back from combat-balance work specifically to "a different layer of design" after three days straight: The Escalator, The Opportunist, The Gambler, The Builder (explicitly Mason philosophy — "I changed the battlefield, deal with it," new design territory he flagged himself rather than me catching it: Zones/Beacons/Hazards as persistent area buffs, e.g. all allies heal/deal/take differently while inside one — no card built yet, parked as its own future subsystem, not a quick add), The Parasite (predation — drains, hijacks, steals; "attack using opponent's Mind" would be a genuine sim first, nothing currently reads a different combatant's own stat), The Collector, The Coward (confirmed as Rasp's own kit generalized into a reusable template, not personal to her), The Judge ("very Tales Untold" — punishes symmetry/pattern, e.g. both Frontlines occupied), The Teacher (an antagonist whose cruelty is *improving* the player mid-fight — rewards reading, punishes sloppy play — genuinely distinct emotional register from the other eight), and The Mirror — refined against The Parasite in the same conversation: Mirror imitates (copies actions/buffs, takes nothing), Parasite predates (drains/hijacks/steals); real distinction, not two names for the same idea.
  - **Also a real, generalizable design point about costs, separate from the archetypes themselves:** current HP-cost cards under-use the texture available — Exhaust-seeding into your own hand reads as genuine overexertion (though it's worth knowing Exhaust's seeding/removal has never been implemented in the sim at all, glossary-only right now), and *gating* itself is a cost, not just payment — RETALIATE (from earlier tonight) is the exemplar: a card can be "expensive" purely through how rarely its trigger lines up, with the payoff scaled to match, no HP or stat spent at all. Flagged one proposal as a genuine Authority-3 departure rather than folding it in quietly: "lose 1 Body until next short rest" would be the first stat cost to survive past combat's end, when every current stat-drain card (Sunder/Wither/Erode) explicitly reverts at combat close by rule — not decided here, just named as bigger than it reads.
  - **First two cards built from the list, both Mirror, both Red:** STARING CONTEST — actually implemented for the first time (previously registered with zero effect/defense, flagged as needing an engine hook distinct from Initiative Shift X — "move to immediately follow a chosen token" is a direct requeue, not a numeric shift). Drew's explicit call, not a default: keep the card's original mechanic rather than replace it, since a Mirror-archetype build should touch all three pillars (RPS/Initiative/Position) or have a stated reason not to, and Staring Contest's requeue is a genuinely unique way to touch the Initiative pillar worth keeping on its own terms. New engine primitive built for it — `_reposition_after` (`engine.py`), a plain list reorder deliberately NOT routed through `_apply_shift`'s bonus-turn/shift-skip machinery, since the card's own text never says "Initiative Shift." Verified safe to call even on the currently-resolving actor: `_rotate_current`'s existing "no-op if queue[0] isn't who anymore" guard, built for an unrelated case, already covers exactly this situation. **WAITING GAME** — a new card, the actual copy-a-buff Mirror mechanic originally drafted for Staring Contest before Drew redirected it: copies one Positive Status Effect (Deadly/Resist/Evade/Fortress, fixed priority order for the bot; Anchored/Quick skipped — Anchored isn't a simple flag, Quick has no implementation anywhere) the opponent currently holds; a real whiff against a bare target, by design. Tested: scripted verification of both mechanics (reposition in 2- and 3-seat queues, status-copy priority order and the no-op case) plus a 5,000-battle stress test with all five of tonight's new cards live in every hand simultaneously — zero crashes. Full regression re-run clean. Print sheets regenerated.
- **A3 — AFTERIMAGE, the game's first colorless card, plus a real bug it exposed in the reveal-color plumbing built for it.** Continuing the Mirror archetype list from the entry above: Drew's concept was a card that copies "the last color used by whoever went the turn before" — deliberately the deeper version over a simpler alternative (mirroring damage/effect numbers instead), since matching the actual color is what makes the card interact honestly with RPS and, just as deliberately, with color-locked bans like Axiom: a card that doesn't know its own color until reveal can't be pre-banned at commitment time the way a fixed-color card can. That's a real Authority-3 call, not an incidental side effect — it changes how "ban a color" reads for one specific card, flagged here rather than folded in quietly. Built via a new `_effective_color(engine, card)` helper (`engine.py`) that reads `engine._prior_turn_hit['color']` (defaulting to Green if no one has acted yet) without ever mutating the shared Card object itself — Cards are singletons referenced by every deck that includes them, so per-reveal state has to live as a local computed value, never written back onto `.color`/`.stat`. Threaded through both engines' `attack()` (every `.color` read site swapped to the computed value) and `rps()`/`_rps()`, with one deliberate exception left reading the native `.color`: the Axiom-ban check itself, which is the whole point — Axiom bans by the card's true native color, and colorless cards have none, so they're categorically exempt rather than accidentally exempt. Damage reads both the mirrored color *and* the mirrored stat, using the caster's own value of that stat, not the original actor's number (`_afterimage_damage`, `content.py`). Effect/Defensive Bonus landed on Blind (Effect) and self-Evade (Defensive Bonus) per Drew's direct correction — first drafted as Deadly/Resist, wrong register for a card about disorienting mimicry rather than power-copying. **Real bug found building it, not caused by it:** `_resolve_attacker_win` in both engines was silently dropping the `'color'` field every time it rebuilt `_this_turn_hit` on a clean win — the field was being set correctly at reveal, then discarded a few lines later the instant the attacker won cleanly, meaning Afterimage would have silently defaulted to Green after almost every decisive hit. Caught by direct testing before trusting the mechanic, not by inspection. Fixed by explicitly carrying `.get('color')` forward in the rebuilt dict, both engines. **Two more real bugs found via stress-testing, both pre-existing AI-heuristic gaps Afterimage was simply the first card to actually trigger:** `policies.py`'s `est_damage()` crashed on `card.stat=None` (the generic `getattr(me, card.stat)` fallback assumes every card has one); the tactician policy's `_value()` crashed on `card.color=None` via `_BEATEN_BY[None]`. Both fixed with a targeted special-case rather than a generic guard everywhere, since Afterimage is currently the only card without a native color/stat. Verified: a 3000-duel stress test (both `reader` and `tactician` policies) and a 5000-battle team stress test with Afterimage live in every hand, zero crashes after fixes; full standing regression suite (all duel and team matchups, Patient Host 1v3) re-run clean against prior baselines, no drift. **New home, not an existing file:** as the first card with no color at all, it doesn't fit any `cards/[color]-[stat].md` file — shipped to a new `cards/colorless.md` instead of forcing it into an existing one, with `printing/generate-cards.py` extended (COLOR_HEX/COLOR_BG/COLOR_LABEL entries, a `COLORLESS`-line parse branch alongside the existing `BLUE|RED|GREEN` regex) to render it. Print sheets regenerated (93 items / 11 pages, up from 92). Veto reverts.
- **A1 — tactician's colorless-card heuristic upgraded from a skip-guard to a real read; WAITING GAME buffed to copy up to two statuses, opening room for Parasite's steal-1 card.** Two small, unrelated fixes/changes shipped together the same pass. (1) The `est_damage`/tactician crash-guards from AFTERIMAGE's build were only avoidance, not a real answer to Drew's direct question — "what would an actual heuristic used by a human look like?" The honest answer: a human at the table already knows what Afterimage will mirror by the time they're deciding whether to play it, since the prior turn already resolved and revealed before this turn's choice is made — colorless-until-reveal is a fact about the card's *face*, not about what's knowable at decision time. So the tactician's risk-free-attack read (is the foe's counter-color exhausted?) now computes the mirrored color via `_effective_color(engine, card)` before the lookup, instead of skipping the check outright on `card.color is None` — same check every other card gets, one extra step. `est_damage()` was left as its average-of-three-stats approximation, not upgraded to the same precision: its signature (`me, card`) never carries `engine`, and most of its call sites (Scry evaluating cards still buried in a deck) don't have a stable "immediately before" to read anyway — threading `engine` through every call site for one card's damage estimate would be a real refactor, not asked for. Verified: a scripted test forcing a known prior-turn color and toggling the foe's counter-color exhaustion confirms the +2 bump now tracks the *mirrored* color, not the (always-None) printed one; full regression (frost/steele, mire/volk both reader and tactician, sage/adept, tempo/warden, vanguard/mire and Patient Host 1v3 team battles) re-run clean, plus a fresh 3,000-duel tactician-vs-tactician stress test on an Afterimage-heavy deck, zero crashes. (2) WAITING GAME's copy widened from one Positive Status Effect to up to two different ones (same fixed priority order — Deadly > Resist > Evade > Fortress — capped to what's actually present, never fabricating a second), freeing "steal exactly 1" as Parasite's own distinct number rather than colliding with Waiting Game's existing copy-1 — Drew: "waiting game should copy up to 2 because parasite should get a card that steals 1." `content.py`'s `_copy_one_status` renamed `_copy_statuses(me, foe, count=2)`; `cards/red-body.md` updated on both Effect and Defensive Bonus lines. Verified with a direct script (two-status foe copies exactly the top two by priority, one-status foe copies just the one, zero-status foe whiffs cleanly) plus the same full regression suite, clean. Print sheets regenerated. (3) `cards/colorless.md`'s scope note broadened per Drew, half-joking that I'd already built the file he was about to ask for: it's now framed as the home for any card without a fixed color/stat, not just colorless-until-reveal cards specifically — flagged as the future home for status-effect-only cards too, no such card built yet. Veto reverts.
- **A3 — FOLLOW-UP (Mirror, colorless), DRAIN and CONSUME (Parasite's first two real cards), and a genuine new engine mechanism (`_stamp_reveal`/`_resolve_follow_up`) built to support the first one.** Continuing straight down the Mirror/Parasite build list. Two clarifying answers from Drew locked the design before any code was written: FOLLOW-UP's empty case (no ally has revealed yet) is a flat **auto-lose** — not a weak fallback like AFTERIMAGE's default-to-Green, a guaranteed loss of the reveal — and a status card landing on top of the target ally's pile is ruled the *same* no-op, not a "skip to the next real card underneath." CONSUME's destroy-a-card cost is **mandatory**, matching SACRIFICE STRIKE's existing always-fires pattern rather than inventing a new optional-cost decision point.
  - **FOLLOW-UP is a stronger transformation than AFTERIMAGE, not a variant of it** — it doesn't just borrow a color, it fully BECOMES a copy of whatever an ally most recently revealed (attack or defense), Effect/Defensive Bonus/damage all replaced by the copied card's own — so it needed real new plumbing, not a reuse of `_effective_color`. Built: `_stamp_reveal(engine, who, card)` (a monotonic `engine._reveal_seq` clock, stamped on every attack/defense reveal — deliberately scoped to *revealed* cards only, not every possible discard source in the codebase — Scry-bin discards, forced hand-size trims, etc. — since instrumenting all ~19 of those call sites across two engines and a dozen unrelated card effects, several without engine access at all, would be real scope creep for a case — a status card surfacing as a target — that's structurally impossible today anyway, since status cards are never legal attack/defense picks) and `_resolve_follow_up(engine, actor)` (looks at `engine.allies(actor)` — never `actor` itself, matching the WARSONG convention, so FOLLOW-UP is a guaranteed dead card in a 1v1 Duel by construction, `Duel.allies()` always returning `[]` — and picks whichever has the highest `_last_reveal_seq`).
  - **`attack()` in both engines now tracks two separate references per side** — `physical_card`/`physical_def_card` (the true card that leaves hand and returns to *the copier's own* discard, per Drew's confirmed spec: "FOLLOW-UP itself, not the copied card's name") versus `card`/`def_card` (reassigned to the resolved copy target, used for every color/effect/damage/display purpose from that point on). A chain of FOLLOW-UPs correctly resolves through each other to the underlying real card, never to the literal string "FOLLOW-UP" — `_stamp_reveal` always records the *resolved* card, since that's what's actually visible at the table, not an invisible pass-through label. Verified directly: ally reveals STRIKE, teammate's FOLLOW-UP copies it (real damage lands, teammate's own discard still shows FOLLOW-UP), a third teammate's FOLLOW-UP then chain-copies through the second one's resolved identity (lands real STRIKE damage too, not a dead card).
  - **A real, deliberate design difference from AFTERIMAGE: FOLLOW-UP does NOT get Axiom immunity.** AFTERIMAGE is colorless *by design* specifically to dodge color-locked bans (Drew's stated intent); FOLLOW-UP isn't dodging anything — it fully becomes the copied card, so it's exactly as bannable as if that card had been played directly. This falls out of the code naturally (the Axiom-ban check reads the *resolved* `def_card.color`, unlike AFTERIMAGE's deliberate carve-out reading the native color) rather than needing a special case — flagged here as a real Authority-2 call, not an assumption, since it's the kind of thing that changes how "ban a color" reads for a second card now. Verified directly: a defender with `axiom_ban='R'` whose FOLLOW-UP resolves into a Red copy gets it banned exactly like any other Red pick — a free takeback (card returns to hand, nothing spent), distinct from FOLLOW-UP's own genuine dead-case (spent, no takeback) — matching the illegal-defense-mistake ruling already in `rules/combat.md` for the Axiom case specifically.
  - **Both the "committed but dead" cases are genuinely spent, not free takebacks** — an attacker whose FOLLOW-UP has nothing to copy still discards it and wastes the turn; a defender who commits FOLLOW-UP as their defense and it resolves dead still has it removed from hand and discarded, then resolves as no legal defense (attacker wins fully uncontested) — the same "real whiff, by the card's own design" shape WAITING GAME already established against a buff-less target, not a mistake deserving a mulligan. Verified directly, both roles, both engines.
  - **DRAIN** (Red, Body + d2): Parasite's real predation card, distinguished from Mirror's WAITING GAME by actually removing the stolen status from the foe rather than just copying it — the exact Mirror-vs-Parasite line Drew drew when the two archetypes were first named. Refactored `_copy_statuses` into a shared `_transfer_statuses(me, foe, count, steal)` so WAITING GAME (steal=False) and DRAIN (steal=True, count=1) share one fixed-priority implementation instead of duplicating it.
  - **CONSUME** (Green, Soul + d4): the generic "destroy a card for power" seed Drew flagged and parked earlier this session ("just having the odds of hitting a stolen card happens are enough"), generalized to your own hand instead of requiring a stolen one. Full-damage Lifesteal (not BLOOD IN THE GAP's existing half-damage version — a real, deliberate difference, matching Consume's higher cost) plus a mandatory destroy-1-from-own-hand (routed to `exile`, genuinely permanent, never `discard` — confirmed the destroyed card can't return even on a mid-combat reshuffle) that inflicts Weak + Blind on the opponent. The Defensive Bonus rolls its own fresh Soul + d4 counter-damage (same shape as SACRIFICE STRIKE's Counter Attack) so Lifesteal has something real to heal off on the defense side too, since CONSUME's printed Attack line only pays out automatically via the Effect side. An empty hand (nothing left to destroy) just skips the destroy+debuff cleanly, no crash, no special-casing needed. Destroyed card picked uniformly at random from the remaining hand, matching FORGET's existing convention for "remove a card from hand" rather than inventing a new selection heuristic.
  - **AI-heuristic crash risk checked proactively this time, not found the hard way:** `est_damage()` extended to cover FOLLOW-UP alongside AFTERIMAGE in the same `card.stat is None` special case (same average-of-three-stats placeholder) — checked every other `card.color`/`card.stat` read site in `policies.py` first and confirmed the rest are plain equality comparisons, safe against `None` without crashing.
  - **Tested thoroughly before trusting:** scripted verification of DRAIN (steals exactly the top-priority status, decrements the foe, toggles Fortress rather than duplicating it, whiffs cleanly against a bare target) and CONSUME (both roles, full-damage lifesteal, exile-not-discard, Weak+Blind applied, empty-hand whiff) in isolation; a full team-battle walkthrough of FOLLOW-UP covering the successful copy, both auto-lose roles (attacker and defender), the guaranteed-dead Duel case, the chain-through-another-FOLLOW-UP case, and the Axiom-ban-by-resolved-color case — nine targeted scenarios, all passed on the first fully-corrected run. Then a 3,000-battle team stress test and a 3,000-duel stress test with all three cards live in every hand, zero crashes; full standing regression suite (all duel and team matchups, Patient Host 1v3) re-run clean against prior baselines, no drift (expected — no existing roster deck includes any of the three yet). Card text added to `cards/colorless.md` (FOLLOW-UP), `cards/red-body.md` (DRAIN), `cards/green-soul.md` (CONSUME); print sheets regenerated (96 items / 11 pages, up from 93). Veto reverts.
  - **Still open, not decided here:** the actual steal-a-card-from-hand-and-return-it-at-combat's-end Parasite card (needs new cross-hand-transfer and combat-end-return infrastructure, neither built yet) is still unnamed and unbuilt — naming suggestions offered (HOSTAGE, POACH, HIJACK, SNATCH), no pick confirmed yet.
- **A3 — AFTERIMAGE's Green default removed; it now auto-loses with nothing to mirror, same as FOLLOW-UP.** A real correction to already-shipped, already-tested canon, caught by Drew directly: "afterimage should remain colorless the same way as followup. not default to green." AFTERIMAGE's original build (two entries above) had `_effective_color` fall back to `'G'` whenever `_prior_turn_hit` was empty — reasonable-looking at the time, but inconsistent with the design principle FOLLOW-UP made explicit later the same session: colorless-until-reveal means genuinely no color if there's nothing to show, not a soft default. Fixed at the root: `_effective_color`'s `mirror_color` branch now returns `engine._prior_turn_hit.get('color')` bare (`None` when empty), no `or 'G'`. `attack()` in both engines gained one shared check right after computing `atk_color` (and the mirrored equivalent on the defender's reveal path): if it's `None`, auto-lose the reveal outright — the exact same branch FOLLOW-UP's own empty case already used, so the fix actually *simplified* the code (FOLLOW-UP's dead-Card-object branch and AFTERIMAGE's dead-color branch collapsed into one check, since an unresolved FOLLOW-UP's native color is also `None`). `_afterimage_damage` no longer needs its own `or 'G'` fallback either — that function only ever runs past the new dead-check, so a real color is guaranteed by the time it's called. Card text corrected (`cards/colorless.md`): "defaults to Green/Soul if no one has" replaced with "auto-loses the reveal outright." Verified directly: AFTERIMAGE with no prior reveal now deals zero damage and wastes the turn (confirmed NOT the old default-Green attack); AFTERIMAGE with a real prior color still works exactly as before. Re-ran every FOLLOW-UP scenario from the entry above to confirm the shared-branch refactor didn't disturb any of them (successful copy, both auto-lose roles, guaranteed-dead Duel case) — all still pass. Full 3,000-battle/3,000-duel stress test with AFTERIMAGE+FOLLOW-UP+DRAIN+CONSUME all live, and the complete standing regression suite, re-run clean against prior baselines. Print sheets regenerated. Veto reverts.
- **HIJACK locked in as the name for the still-unbuilt steal-a-card Parasite card** (final answer, Drew's words) — HOSTAGE dropped for implying kidnapping a real person, too heavy a connotation for what's meant to be a clean card-game steal. Amusing side note worth keeping for the record: Drew initially misread my own "HIJACK" suggestion as "HUACK" and asked where that word came from, suspecting a hallucination or a hidden repo reference — it wasn't either; HIJACK was one of four names I'd suggested, quoting his own earlier description of the Parasite archetype ("draining, hijacking, stealing"), and HUACK was simply a font-rendering near-miss on the same word. Card itself still needs the cross-hand-transfer + combat-end-return infrastructure built (nothing shipped here, naming only).
- **A3 — Mason Glyphs, a genuinely new subsystem: the Builder archetype's first real cards, and the first content this session to introduce a targetable, non-combatant battlefield entity.** Ties directly to already-established canon rather than inventing from nothing — `factions/the-masons.md`'s Known Works already lists "Stabilization glyphs — carved into cliff faces at key locations to anchor held reality against Unheld pressure" as a Mason Method; these four cards are a combat-scale mechanical expression of that same craft, not a claim that they ARE the world-level Unheld wards (left deliberately unconnected in the flavor text, matching Translation Principle's redefinition test — that specific claim was never made and isn't asserted here). Built entirely through a real design conversation, not assumed — full trail, since the sign-off IS the conversation:
  - **The core mechanic, settled answer by answer:** a glyph is carved at whatever position (Frontline/Backline) the Mason occupied at creation and stays fixed there — "the fiction is they are destroying the glyph not the structure itself. the object isn't something that pops into existence in the fiction. it's the mason marking what's already in the environment." It **outlasts the Mason who carved it** (not tied to their continued presence — a real standing battlefield fixture, not an extension of their turn). **Any hit destroys it outright** — no threshold, no HP, no roll: "against the glyph attacks don't trigger effect or roll for damage. they just destroy the glyph and get discarded." Its own passive **ticks for free** every turn for whoever occupies its position — no roll, no contest, an ambient rule of the battlefield rather than a combat action. **Fortress protects a glyph exactly as written** for allies — Drew: "fortress the existing status. it redirects attack from the object to whoever has the fortress status" — a genuine, explicit extension of Fortress's scope (`rules/card-glossary.md`: "the next time an ally would take attack damage, you take it instead") to cover "an ally or your side's glyph," flagged here precisely rather than silently reused, since it changes what "ally" covers for one specific status.
  - **Four glyphs built from Drew's own named list**, not invented: heal 1/turn, +1 damage/turn, Thorns, Obscure ("obscure is amazing here 🤩"). Colors assigned by existing precedent, not guessed: Green/Soul for the heal (matches WARDEN's established green-support-anchor role), Red/Body for both the damage buff and Thorns (checked first — Thorns is already granted by a Red card elsewhere in the deck, `cards/red-body.md`), Blue/Mind for Obscure (information/concealment, matching Blue's established register). MASON is an already-established tag in `CLAUDE.md`'s own tag examples, confirming these are meant as player-obtainable faction-sourced content (acquisition lineage), not GM-only kit, despite the Masons themselves being a secret faction — the secrecy applies to their existence and Known Works, not to whether a PC can carry a card traceable to them.
  - **Shipped as real canon card text**, not experimental: MENDING GLYPH, HONING GLYPH, BARBED GLYPH, CIPHER GLYPH, new file `cards/mason-glyphs.md` (a new file within the already-established `cards/` directory, same footing as `colorless.md`), with the shared glyph rules stated once at the top rather than repeated on all four cards.
  - **Deliberately NOT built this pass: any combat-simulator support.** This needed real new architecture the size of (likely bigger than) FOLLOW-UP's own build — a new engine-level entity type (glyphs aren't Combatants, so none of the existing target/attack/policy machinery applies to them as-is), a new AI decision surface (every policy would need to learn "attack the glyph instead of a combatant" as a real option), a team+position-keyed ambient-tick hook (reusable from `_ongoing_support_tick`'s existing per-turn pattern, but still new wiring), and the Fortress-redirect extension actually implemented in `warded()`/`deal()`. Given the size of that commitment, this shipped as real, playable-at-the-table canon text now (Working mode: ship the target file, record the dependent as stale) rather than silently building or silently skipping the sim side. Logged under Pending propagation below. **Confirmed:** "let's wait for a Sync tomorrow to wire in the mason cards to the sim" — standing as queued, not decided to skip.
  - **Noted, not acted on:** Drew mentioned a separate, later batch of colorless cards tied to the People of Promise and Kaine — explicitly flagged by him as "separate ideas for later," not detailed yet, nothing to build or decide here.
- **A3 — Colorless generalized into a real, stated rule, correcting the ad-hoc "auto-lose with nothing to mirror" behavior FOLLOW-UP and AFTERIMAGE both shipped with; BECOMING (the Oracle-drafting card) built alongside it.** Drew's own words, precise enough to be the actual rule rather than a vibe: "colorless cards auto lose to any color. they don't auto lose against someone who doesn't/can't block and it doesn't lose to someone collapsed. it only loses when it's challenged by a card with an actual color." This is a real correction, not just new scope — my earlier FOLLOW-UP/AFTERIMAGE builds made "nothing to mirror" an unconditional whiff (an early return before ever checking whether the defender could even respond), which was wrong: a colorless card should still win an uncontested reveal fully, exactly like any other card would.
  - **The fix actually simplified the code rather than growing it.** `_rps_base` (both engines) gained the real rule directly: `atk == dfn` still covers colorless-vs-colorless as a tie (`None == None`), and two new lines handle colorless-vs-real symmetrically (`atk is None -> 'defender'`, `dfn is None -> 'attacker'`) before the normal RPS triangle check — colorless auto-loses whichever side it's on, never wins, only ties another colorless. That one fix let the entire "auto-lose early return" block in `attack()` (both engines) get deleted outright, since attack()'s existing uncontested-win paths (no defense, Collapsed, Staggered) already skip `rps()` entirely and were never the problem — only the color-vs-color comparison needed fixing. The FOLLOW-UP-specific "committed but dead, no legal defense" branch also became fully unreachable and was deleted the same way: a defender's FOLLOW-UP with nothing to copy now just stays FOLLOW-UP's own native (colorless) identity and flows through `rps()` normally, instead of being forced to `None`.
  - **Damage still needed real fixes, since the reveal can now legitimately succeed with nothing to mirror.** `_afterimage_damage` now returns a bare `roll(4, rng)` (no stat bonus) when there's nothing to mirror, instead of crashing on `COLOR_TO_STAT[None]` — an honest reading of "mirrors X + d4" when there's no X. FOLLOW-UP got its own `_follow_up_damage` (flat 0) for the same reachable-again case, since unlike AFTERIMAGE it has no printed formula of its own to fall back on at all.
  - **A real, if minor, crash risk found proactively while auditing for this**, not the hard way: `policies.py`'s `ReaderPolicy.choose_action` read `me.attack_history.most_common(1)[0][0]` directly into `_BEATEN_BY[...]` with no None-guard — now that a colorless reveal can legitimately top a combatant's own attack history, this was a live `_BEATEN_BY[None]` KeyError waiting to happen. Fixed with a new shared helper, `_most_common_real_color(history)` — deliberately checks `.most_common(1)[0][0]` FIRST and only falls back to a filtered scan if that top entry is actually `None`, to avoid quietly changing tie-breaking behavior for every other, unrelated matchup (`Counter.most_common(n)` and the no-arg full-list form aren't provably interchangeable without checking, so this preserves the exact existing behavior in the overwhelmingly common case and only touches what's actually new). Also cleaned up two soft (non-crashing) gaps in the same audit: `GreedyPolicy`/`TacticianPolicy`'s `name_axiom_color` could previously "choose" to ban colorless, which Axiom's own ban check silently treats as no ban at all (`if defender.axiom_ban:` — `None` is falsy) — a wasted card effect, not a crash, now skipped via the same helper.
  - **BECOMING, a new colorless card, built alongside the fix rather than separately** — Drew's spec: "choose a card in hand permanently replace it with a card from the Oracle deck. exile this card until end of combat. permanently destroy this card after 3 uses." Checked `testcampaigndecks/oracle.md` and `rules/gm-guide.md` before writing anything: the Oracle is a real, established end-of-session table ritual ("a question answered, a card glimpsed and buried, a pick from three") — session-persistent, entirely outside any single combat's scope. BECOMING is real canon text (`cards/colorless.md`), but its actual mechanic is deliberately unmodeled in the simulator, same treatment as PREDICT/VOID's item-usage gaps — the Oracle pool has no representation in `combatsimulations/` at all, and the 3-uses-then-permanently-destroyed counter is table/character-sheet bookkeeping that survives across multiple combats, which this engine has never tracked (same reasoning as why an HP cost surviving past combat's end was flagged, not built, earlier this session). Registered with a safe zero-damage function (`_becoming_damage`) so it's a real, legal (if inert) colorless card rather than a crash risk — `est_damage()` extended to return 0 for it directly, matching its own "pure utility" nature rather than lumping it in with AFTERIMAGE/FOLLOW-UP's damage-estimate case.
  - **Verified thoroughly**, since this touched load-bearing RPS logic shipped and tested earlier the same session: direct tests of `_rps_base` (colorless-vs-colorless tie, colorless-vs-real both roles, normal RPS unaffected) in both engines; AFTERIMAGE and FOLLOW-UP each tested with nothing to mirror in all three real scenarios — uncontested win (succeeds, real/zero damage respectively), challenged by a real color (loses cleanly), challenged by another colorless card (ties) — plus re-verification that the successful-copy, chain-copy, and Axiom-ban-on-a-resolved-color cases from FOLLOW-UP's original build still all pass unchanged. `_most_common_real_color` tested directly against a colorless-topped Counter. Then a 5,000-battle and 5,000-duel (all five policies, including punisher for the first time against this card set) stress test with every colorless card live in every hand, zero crashes; full standing regression suite re-run and confirmed byte-for-byte identical to the pre-existing baseline (double-checked via `git stash` against the last commit directly, after an initial scare from misremembering which policy pairing owned which number — tactician-vs-tactician's figures, not reader-vs-reader's, were what I'd been comparing against by mistake). Card text corrected in `cards/colorless.md` (both AFTERIMAGE and FOLLOW-UP's "auto-loses the reveal outright" lines were wrong under the new rule, now read "stays genuinely colorless"), a new shared rule paragraph added at the top of the file, print sheets regenerated (97 items / 11 pages). Veto reverts.
- **A2 ⚑ two new archetypes named (Cultivator, Weaver); SEED built as Cultivator's first card — the "tempo-as-a-resource" gap I'd flagged the morning after, closed for real.** Drew, opening a new session deliberately asking for my own read rather than his: "is there something that you see missing, a behavior that isn't expressed yet?" My answer — nothing spends a player's own future turn as a cost or an investment, only HP/hand-cards/trigger-conditions — turned out to already be close to a shape Drew had independently been circling: **Cultivator** ("plants delayed effects, improves cards instead of immediately benefiting, invests now for larger future turns... likes Builders because both create persistent state," question: "what can I nurture?") and **Weaver** ("makes unrelated mechanics interact... turns two average cards into one powerful interaction," example AXIOM+PARADOX, question: "what can I connect?"). Weaver noted, not yet built toward.
  - **Settled the shape collaboratively, each round a real refinement, not just approval:** first "N turns from now" (my proposal) → Drew simplified to "your next turn, no count tracking" (a real design call, not just a preference — it also made the eventual build cheaper, see below) → then added the Position gate ("if you move position before the start of your next turn it doesn't trigger until you move back into the same position as the seed") — genuinely his own addition, ties the mechanic to a second pillar (Position) on top of tempo, not something I'd suggested → visibility settled decisively, not left ambiguous: "the game's about interaction. it's no fun if the opponent doesn't have a chance to interact" (logged separately above as a standing tiebreaker) → I initially overcorrected the Defensive Bonus to skip the plant-and-wait shape entirely on the theory that "defending is reactive, no time to plant" — Drew caught it directly: "seed doesn't imply that on its own. a player could just say I drop the seed on the ground as part of my block," a real, useful correction (keywords/flavor don't imply a fictional timing constraint the mechanic itself never stated) — Effect and Defensive Bonus both plant the same way, just with a different payoff (Deadly twice vs. Resist 2).
  - **The name wasn't picked at random — SEED already existed, unbuilt, in `characters/rasp.md`'s core-card list** ("SEED — Exile 1 card, deal its damage in retaliation next time this defender damages you"), referencing a Green core card that was never actually built anywhere in `cards/green-soul.md`. Checked her "Combat Identity" write-up before reusing the name for a different mechanic: it never actually leans on Seed specifically (only Quiet Poison/Septic Strike get called out), so nothing about her established identity was riding on the old, unbuilt description — repurposing the name closed a real gap in her sheet as a side effect, not a casualty of it. Her sheet's own SEED line updated to match what actually got built.
  - **A real correction, not a nitpick: Ward has nothing to do with Seed, on two separate counts, not one.** I'd apparently characterized Ward as a possible answer to Seed; Drew: "ward doesn't block buffs. I think we have it coded so that ward prevents effects from enemies. the seed isn't an enemy so why would ward prevent SEED's buff?" Both halves are right independently — Ward (`rules/card-glossary.md`) blocks the next *debuff*, and specifically one applied *to you by someone else*; Seed's payoff is a positive, purely self-applied buff, so it was never in Ward's scope regardless of who's casting it. The real counters, both genuinely live: kill/Collapse the caster before their next turn at that position, or force them off it with Push/Pull/Repel (a delay, not a cancel).
  - **BERSERKER'S PRICE** (`cards/red-body.md`, pre-existing, Red — Body: "Cannot defend until your next turn" for Body+2d6 now) surfaced on request as a real, already-shipped precedent for "the cost lands on your own next turn" — a close cousin to the still-unbuilt Gambler card Drew's asking for next (gain/do X now, skip your draw step next turn), which pays the same kind of debt in a different currency (a card, not a defense window).
  - **Engine: `_ongoing_support_tick` gained its first ever one-shot entry.** Every existing 'anchor'-gated kind there (`dig_in`, `rooted_oath`, `ledger`...) reapplies every turn for as long as the position holds; SEED needed to fire exactly once and then clear, which none of the existing kinds did. Added a `spent` list collected during the tick's loop, filtered out by identity (`is`, not `==`) afterward — deliberately not dict-equality, since two structurally-identical seed entries (same kind/anchor/owner, entirely possible if a combatant plants two of the same flavor) would otherwise both get removed by an equality-based filter even though only one had actually fired. `_seed_effect`/`_seed_defense` (`content.py`) each just append one `ongoing` entry with the casting position recorded as `anchor` — no new Card-level machinery needed at all, confirming the "your next turn, no counter" simplification really did make this the cheapest new mechanic shipped this session.
  - **Tested directly before trusting**: seed plants with the correct anchor position; fires and is consumed when the combatant is still there on their own next tick; stays fully dormant (untouched, not removed) if they've moved away; fires correctly whenever they eventually return, however many turns later; the Defensive Bonus's `seed_resist` variant behaves identically with its own payoff; two independent seeds (one Deadly-flavored, one Resist-flavored) at different positions resolve independently without interfering. Re-verified through the real `attack()` → `start_of_turn()` pipeline, not just the isolated helper. Then a 3,000-duel and 3,000-team-battle stress test with a SEED-heavy deck across all policies, zero crashes; full standing regression suite re-run, confirmed identical to baseline. Card text added to `cards/green-soul.md`, print sheets regenerated (98 items / 11 pages). Veto reverts.
  - **Still open, not built**: the Gambler card (gain/do X now, skip your draw step next turn) — design settled enough to build (mirrors Berserker's Price's shape), just not yet started.
- **Archetypes moved to a permanent home, `agent-tools/archetypes.md`, out of memory.md.** Drew: "let's store these archetypes in a list in the repo somewhere more permanent than memory.md, it's not official canon, more like a design north star." Explicit, load-bearing distinction stated the same breath: "for the actual table I don't want official archetypes, at the table archetypes aren't revealed, they are naturally discovered" — the new file states this directly at the top, since it's the one thing that would actually hurt something if it leaked (an archetype name surfacing to a player breaks the discovery the whole idea depends on). All twelve archetypes named so far (Escalator, Opportunist, Gambler, Builder, Parasite, Collector, Coward, Judge, Teacher, Mirror, Cultivator, Weaver) compiled there with accurate shipped/unbuilt status, plus the three standing design principles that came out of building them this session (keyword-as-resolution-not-identity, telegraph-over-concealment, the three-pillars check). The historical memory.md entries where each archetype was actually named stay exactly as written — this is a compiled reference copied out, not a move that empties the log.
- **A3 — EMERGENCY REPAIRS and OVERCOMMIT shipped; Quick and Exhaust both implemented in the simulator for the first time.** Both had been sitting fully defined in `rules/card-glossary.md` all session, granted by a couple of already-shipped cards, with the engine side flagged as a no-op every time ("Quick has no implementation anywhere yet"). OVERCOMMIT's design (Drew: "gain Deadly, resist, quick, and evade. add 2 exhaust to hand") forced both gaps closed at once, rather than either sitting parked any longer.
  - **EMERGENCY REPAIRS** — the actual Gambler card (Red — Body, ranged), settled through a real back-and-forth: Green/Soul → Drew moved it to **Red**, and the heal formula to **2 × Soul** specifically — a Red card whose own Attack line rolls Body, but whose Effect text reaches across to read Soul directly. Drew's own read, worth keeping verbatim: "now it's really revealing where the cards want to touch other colors." First card this session (maybe ever, not checked exhaustively) to explicitly reference a stat other than its own printed color/stat — a card's color governs its Attack roll, nothing more; nothing stops the Effect text from reading whatever stat the fiction calls for. Cost lands on the caster's own next turn (skip the draw step) — same family as SEED's tempo-investment and BERSERKER'S PRICE's existing "cost on your next turn" precedent, just paid in a resource (a card) instead of a defense window.
  - **OVERCOMMIT** — Gambler's real thesis card: all four Positive Status Effects at once (Deadly, Resist, Quick, Evade), paid for with 2 Exhaust cards seeded directly into hand. A genuinely different cost shape from anything shipped before it — HP (Sacrifice Strike), a single hand card (Consume/Becoming), and a trigger condition (Retaliate) were the only costs that existed; Exhaust is the first cost that keeps *costing* until the caster spends a whole separate future action clearing it, per its own glossary text ("use your action to permanently remove all Exhaust cards from your hand — that's the only way to clear them").
  - **Quick, implemented**: a free reposition on top of the normal action, good for exactly the caster's next turn (`Combatant._quick`, checked and cleared at the top of `take_turn`/`Battle.take_turn`, right after `start_of_turn`). One real interpretation call, flagged rather than silently assumed: the glossary's "you may" is a genuine table choice, but none of the sim's policies have any position-optimizing logic to make that choice with, so it's applied automatically (an unconditional flip) instead of adding a new decision axis to every policy for one small utility grant. Applies even on a Staggered turn — Quick only ever promises the reposition, never the attack Staggered skips.
  - **Exhaust, implemented**: a new status Card (`is_status=True`, same footing as Injury) seeded directly into a target's **hand** via new `insert_exhaust(target, n)` (both engines) — the actual difference from Injury the glossary already draws: Injury enters the deck and has to be drawn before it costs anything, Exhaust costs the hand slot immediately. Clearing needs a new whole-turn action, `('destroy_exhaust',)`, that removes every Exhaust card from hand in one go (not one at a time, unlike `destroy_injury`) — matches the glossary's own "remove ALL Exhaust cards" text exactly. `idle_recovery` (duel policies) and the team-policy fallback both extended to reach for it under the same conservative rule Injury already used: only on a forced-idle turn, never traded for a real attack.
  - **Tested thoroughly**: Quick's free flip verified in isolation (a policy that always Waits, to rule out the main action's own Move choice coincidentally re-flipping it — which is exactly what happened on the first pass with a real RandomPolicy opponent, a test-harness confusion, not an engine bug, self-caught and re-verified cleanly). Exhaust seeding/clearing verified through the real `setup()` → seed → `take_turn()` pipeline, confirming real cards survive `destroy_exhaust` untouched and the removal only clears Exhaust specifically. Skip-draw verified to suppress exactly one draw, then resume normally. Both new cards tested directly (heal formula, statuses granted, Exhaust count) before a 5,000-duel (all five policies) and 5,000-team-battle stress test with everything live in every hand, zero crashes; full standing regression suite re-run, confirmed identical to baseline (expected — no existing roster deck includes any of these). Card text added to `cards/red-body.md`; EXHAUST needed no new card text of its own, since `rules/card-glossary.md` already fully defined it — only the engine side was ever missing. Print sheets regenerated (100 items / 12 pages). Veto reverts.
  - **Still open**: OVERCOMMIT's name isn't fully locked — Drew offered it alongside LIMITLESS and didn't confirm a pick; shipped as OVERCOMMIT with the alternative flagged, easy to rename either way.
- **A3 — the bestiary promotion batch: nine cards promoted to core, Rushdown implemented for real, FRAME-TRAP built, and two real pre-existing bugs caught along the way.** The single largest batch of new engine surface this session — full trail, since a lot of it was genuine back-and-forth refinement, not a straight build.
  - **Rushdown, a structural gap closed.** Fully defined as a table action since before this session (`rules/combat.md`: "move a Backline enemy to the Frontline, user must be in the Frontline"), referenced by name in bestiary cards, implemented nowhere at all — Drew: "crazy that's slipped by for so long." New shared primitive `_rushdown(actor, target)` (engine.py), a plain no-op if either precondition fails, same shape as every other conditional forced-reposition effect already in `content.py`.
  - **Quick corrected to actually offer Rushdown, not just an auto-flip.** Drew's catch: "quick should grant rushdown or movement. and rushdown isn't really a keyword, it's part of normal movement options" — Rushdown was never a status to grant, it's one of the normal repositioning actions, same footing as Move Position itself. Quick's free action now uses Rushdown automatically when the actor is already Frontline with a Backline enemy to drag in (the more aggressive default), otherwise falls back to the plain self-flip — same "no policy has real decision logic for this" simplification already flagged for Quick's first build, just widened to cover the real choice space now that Rushdown exists.
  - **CERTAIN CONTACT** (was NEVER LIFTED, Wall-Reader) — Drew's addition on promotion: also ignores Resist and Blind, not just Evade. Built as a small, genuinely generic Card-level property, `ignores` (a frozenset of `{'evade','resist','blind'}`), checked directly at the three relevant points (attack()'s Blind/Evade checks, deal()'s Resist reduction) rather than a name-specific special case — in each ignored case, the stack isn't even consumed, not just prevented from triggering (a real, deliberate reading of "cannot be affected by," not "affected but harmlessly").
  - **HEAVE AND HAUL** (was TUNNEL KNOWLEDGE, Borrower) — Drew wanted a less "blunt and heavy-handed" name; landed on this one (not literally "Lefty's," despite the inspiration — flagged as my own reading, since Lefty already has an unrelated HOOK AND HAUL doing a different push/pull shape). Effect always targets Backline (no policy has real "which position" decision logic — matches Quick's own precedent). Defensive Bonus reuses Quick's exact mechanism for "may change position freely" (`_quick = True` on self and all allies) instead of an immediate auto-flip — a real deferred choice, not a forced one right now.
  - **OFF BALANCE** (was YOU CHANGED WALLS, Wall-Reader) — reworked on promotion, not just renamed. Effect switched to "moved" (Drew's own call — the cleaner phrasing) via a brand-new tracker, `_repositioned_since_last_turn`, computed once at the very top of every `take_turn` by comparing current position against a snapshot taken at the start of the combatant's own last turn — the first time "did X reposition since their last turn" has ever been tracked in the sim, needed by this card and ROLLOUT both. Defensive Bonus is a genuinely new condition: gain Resist if the attacker received a positive Initiative Shift or used Wait since their own last turn — two new self-clearing flags (`_shifted_positive`, set inside `_apply_shift`; `_used_wait`, set wherever a policy Waits), cleared at the top of `take_turn` the same way `_anticipating`/`_weathered` already are. **Flagged, not silently assumed:** "since your last turn" reads most literally as bound to the DEFENDER's own timeline; the actual implementation is bound to the AFFECTED combatant's own timeline instead (self-clearing on their own next turn) — a materially simpler, self-contained approximation of the same intent, not a literal cross-combatant comparison.
  - **IRON GRIP** (was CORRECTION GRIP, Alignment Marshal) and **PATIENCE OF STONE** (Delve Roller / Stonecoil, identical in both files already) — both unchanged mechanically, sharing a new `anchor_heal2` ongoing kind (heals 2, not Ledger's 3) since neither creature's "heal 2 while Anchored" shape existed as a reusable primitive before. Drew, on Patience of Stone's asymmetry (gated Effect, unconditional Defensive-Bonus Deadly): "maybe that's okay though" — left exactly as described, not smoothed over.
  - **ROLLOUT** (Delve Roller) — the Pokemon reference, made real: "should go back to hand after used." New Card-level property, `returns_to_hand`, checked at every discard-append point in `attack()` (both engines, four call sites each) via a new shared helper `_discard_or_return` — regardless of outcome (win, tie, or loss), verified directly for both. Needed its own damage function since the +4-if-you-didn't-reposition bonus reuses OFF BALANCE's new tracker, just checking yourself instead of the target.
  - **SEISMIC REDIRECT** (Alignment Marshal) and **GORE** (Minotaur) — unchanged; Seismic Redirect is the first core card to actually call the new `_rushdown` primitive.
  - **YOU'RE NEXT** (was a Patient Host signature, now core) — Drew: "just change the effect so that it only triggers on a win." Gate added (`if me._tie: return`) using the existing `attacker._tie` flag (set True only while an Effect resolves during a tie, `rps()`'s tie branch) — **a real pre-existing gap caught in the process**: `_tie` had never actually been initialized in `Combatant.__init__`, only ever set inside the tie branch itself; reading it on a perfectly ordinary win, before any tie had ever happened to that combatant all match, would have thrown `AttributeError`. Fixed by initializing `self._tie = False` alongside the other reveal-state flags.
  - **FRAME-TRAP**, the most structurally novel card in the batch — Drew's full spec, confirmed word-for-word on the one real ambiguity ("only when hit by an attack, not thorns or anything else"): if the defender was hit by an attack on the turn immediately before this one (`_prior_turn_hit['hit']` and `['target'] is defender` — the exact same "immediately before" convention as Retaliate/Warsong/Rebuttal, not a wider window), this attack auto-wins outright, no RPS at all, and the defender's already-committed, already-revealed card is discarded without its own Defensive Bonus ever resolving. Checked directly in `attack()`, right after the defender's card is revealed but before `rps()` is ever called — not a "no legal defense" case, since the defender genuinely chose and revealed a real card, it's specifically negated after the fact. **Defensive Bonus ("wins ties. on ties negate attacker's effect") turned out to need no separate mechanism at all**: FRAME-TRAP was simply added to `rps()`'s existing `def_wins_tie` name-check (alongside EQUAL FOOTING, Defensive-Bonus-only per its own text, same asymmetric shape as ADAPT's attacker-only claim) — converting a tie to a clean defender win *already* means the attacker's Effect never fires at all in the existing `outcome == 'defender'` branch, which only ever calls the defender's own `defense()`. "Negate the attacker's effect" wasn't a second thing to build; it was already the natural consequence of "wins ties." Also surfaced, not fixed (out of scope): **DEAD HEAT** (`cards/red-body.md`) — the existing "cancels the defender's Defensive Bonus on a tie" card — was never actually implemented in the sim either, canon text only, the same category of gap FRAME-TRAP needed solved, just pointed the other direction.
  - **Tested exhaustively before trusting any of it**, given the sheer surface area: every mechanism above got its own direct scripted test (Rushdown's legality gate both ways, Quick's Rushdown-vs-fallback choice, all three CERTAIN CONTACT bypasses individually with stack-untouched verification, HEAVE AND HAUL's mass-reposition and Quick-grant, `_repositioned_since_last_turn`'s three-turn detect/reset cycle, OFF BALANCE's both conditions and the real `_apply_shift`-triggered flag, IRON GRIP/PATIENCE OF STONE's shared ongoing kind, ROLLOUT's damage formula against 2000-sample averages plus its return-to-hand on both a win and a genuine loss, SEISMIC REDIRECT/GORE by formula, YOU'RE NEXT's tie-gate, and FRAME-TRAP's full matrix — auto-win when hit last turn, no auto-win without the trigger, no auto-win from non-attack damage, and the Defensive-Bonus tie-win with an observable attacker Effect (Rebuttal's Stagger) confirmed genuinely suppressed, not just untested). Then a 5,000-duel (all five policies) and 5,000-team-battle stress test with literally every card from this batch live in every hand at once, zero crashes; full standing regression suite re-run and confirmed byte-for-byte identical to the pre-existing baseline. Card text written to `cards/red-body.md`, `cards/green-soul.md`, `cards/blue-mind.md`; every promoted creature's own card file (`wall-reader.md`, `borrower-hollow.md`, `alignment-marshal-engine.md`, `delve-roller-hollow.md`, `stonecoil-hollow.md`, `the-patient-host.md`, `minotaur-coil.md`) and bestiary narrative file updated to point at the new core cards instead of duplicating the text, rather than leaving two copies of the same card to quietly drift apart. Print sheets regenerated (110 items / 13 pages, up from 100). Veto reverts.
  - **Explicitly cut, not silently dropped:** STILL GROUND (Stonecoil) — Drew: "severely underpowered and basic," no fix proposed, read as a cut rather than asking for a buff; flagged plainly in case that reading is wrong. CLAY BOWL (Borrower) was cut earlier in the same review, before this batch, for being the weakest candidate.
  - **Noticed, not touched (explicitly out of scope):** `cards/alignment-marshal-engine.md`'s CORRECTION LOAD shuffles Exhaust into the target's *deck*, which doesn't match the glossary's actual rule (Exhaust goes directly to hand, unlike Injury) — a pre-existing inconsistency, flagged in that file directly rather than silently fixed, since it wasn't part of what was asked.
  - **Deliberately parked, not decided against:** raising every base attack die by 2 (d2→d4, d4→d6, etc.) to speed up combat — Drew's own instinct, correctly held back ("we definitely don't want to make it lightly"). Worth remembering precisely: every step-up adds exactly +1 average damage regardless of die size, so it doesn't fight "Red's power is inherent" (the absolute gap between colors stays fixed) — what it actually moves is the global damage-to-HP ratio, since HP formulas wouldn't shift with it. A real, cheap next step if this comes back: a reader-vs-reader tournament at the current dice vs. the shifted ones, compared directly, rather than deciding from intuition.
- **A2/A3 — two renames, a genuine keyword system generalized, and three new cards (Critical/EXPOSED, UNMAKE, LEVEL THE FIELD).**
  - **Two renames, both real corrections, not cosmetic.** SEED → **STAKE**: "seed is too specific. the card could reasonably go in a construct themed deck or a mason deck if it didn't have the plantlife lens forced." Same reasoning extended to Mason's own "Glyph" framing — `cards/mason-glyphs.md`'s shared rule-text now describes the underlying mechanic as an **Object** (a persistent, position-anchored battlefield entity), with Mason's own cards kept as Mason's own flavor (a Mason's object legitimately *is* a glyph, same as any other creature keeping its own naming) rather than renaming MENDING GLYPH/HONING GLYPH/BARBED GLYPH/CIPHER GLYPH themselves — flagged as my own reading, not confirmed, easy to extend to the individual card names too if that's what was actually wanted. OFF BALANCE → **TELLS**: "isn't the right name because it implies it has something to do with staggered when it doesn't" — the card was never about Staggered at all (Deadly if the defender moved, Resist if the attacker shifted or waited), a real, deserved catch. Both renames done properly, not just in the card text: registration names, internal `ongoing` kind strings (`seed_deadly`→`stake_deadly` etc.), function names, and every cross-reference in `characters/rasp.md` and the Wall-Reader bestiary files, verified with a direct re-test after the rename that both cards still fire correctly under their new names.
  - **Critical, a new keyword** (`rules/card-glossary.md`): doubles an attack's base damage (stat + die, Deadly/Weak already folded in) before any other bonus applies — not a status anyone holds, each card that grants it states its own trigger. **EXPOSED** (Blue) is the first: Critical if the defender is Staggered. Gated by construction, not by a separate cost layered on afterward — the trigger needs an already-Staggered target (real setup elsewhere, Staggered doesn't just happen) and the base die is kept low (d2) so the multiplier is doing all the work, not a large floor plus a large multiplier stacked together.
  - **UNMAKE** (Blue) and **LEVEL THE FIELD** (Green) — the buff-removal pair Drew asked for, built on infrastructure that turned out to already exist: `remove_positive_status()` and `warded()` were both already in `content.py`, used by TRACE, just never exposed as their own dedicated cards. UNMAKE is the "biggest one" — wipes every Positive Status Effect from the target **and explicitly ignores Ward**, a real, deliberate exception to the established Debuff/Ward rule (same category as AFTERIMAGE bypassing Axiom or FRAME-TRAP bypassing RPS), paid for with 3 Exhaust (steeper than OVERCOMMIT's 2, since this breaks a standing rule on top of wiping an entire buff set at once). LEVEL THE FIELD is the lighter, team-wide version — strips exactly one Positive Status Effect (fixed priority, same as WAITING GAME/DRAIN) from each enemy, and *does* respect Ward normally, unlike its Blue counterpart. New shared helper `_strip_one_status` (pure removal, no benefit to the caster, same priority order as `_transfer_statuses` but without the copy/steal branching).
  - **A real, pre-existing gap found and fixed in the same pass, not from a bug report:** `remove_positive_status()`'s own docstring said "Quick isn't implemented anywhere in the sim yet" — stale since Quick became real earlier this session (OVERCOMMIT). Fixed to also clear `target._quick`, so UNMAKE and LEVEL THE FIELD correctly strip a pending Quick grant too, not just the four originally-covered statuses.

UNMAKE verified to zero every tracked status while leaving Ward itself completely untouched (not even consumed). LEVEL THE FIELD verified to respect and consume Ward when present, and to strip exactly one highest-priority status when not. Then the standard 5,000-duel/5,000-team-battle stress test with the whole new batch live, zero crashes; full regression suite re-run, confirmed identical to baseline. Reverted cleanly: registration name, `ongoing` kind strings, function names, `characters/rasp.md`'s reference, all back to SEED. The Mason "Glyph"→"Object" rule-text generalization (same entry) was **not** reverted — that's a different kind of change (the shared MECHANIC's own name, not a specific card's flavor), and wasn't called out for reversal.
- **"Wins on ties" cards structurally always suppress the other side's payoff — confirmed, not a FRAME-TRAP-specific mechanism.** Drew asked directly: "if the other guy is getting their effect/bonus that isn't winning the tie" — right instinct, verified empirically rather than just reasoned about: `attack()`'s three-way outcome branch only ever calls ONE side's payoff for a clean 'attacker' or 'defender' result (`_resolve_attacker_win` never touches `def_card.defense()`; a clean defender win never touches `card.effect()`) — both sides only ever fire together on a genuine, unconverted tie. Since every "wins on ties" card (EQUAL FOOTING, ADAPT, now FRAME-TRAP) works by converting the outcome from 'tie' to 'attacker'/'defender' in `rps()` before the branch runs, the suppression was already true for EQUAL FOOTING and ADAPT this whole time — FRAME-TRAP didn't introduce a new rule, it just landed on the same existing structural behavior. Verified directly: IRON GRIP (observable Effect: grants Rooted) into EQUAL FOOTING's defense, same color to force a tie — Rooted did NOT apply; the identical matchup with a plain IRON GRIP instead of EQUAL FOOTING on defense DID apply Rooted, confirming the suppression is specific to a tie actually being converted, not a general damping of ties.

Drew: "let's build both the weak and the -shift variants" — both enemy-facing this time, not ally-facing like Mending/Honing/Barbed/Cipher. WITHERING GLYPH (Green — the only color previously covered just once, by Mending): any enemy who takes their turn there gains Weak. MIRING GLYPH (Blue, joining Cipher to balance the color spread at 2/2/2 across the six glyphs): any enemy who takes their turn there suffers Initiative Shift -1. The shared rule-text at the top of `cards/mason-glyphs.md` already said "whoever occupies its position," not "any ally" — the ally-only framing lived entirely in each individual card's own text, so no change was needed there; these two just wrote "enemy" instead. - **[2026-07-21 5:57 PM CDT] Sync pass — the Object subsystem's combat-simulator support built, closing the gap queued since Mason Glyphs first shipped.** Drew: "go ahead and sync pass." All six Mason Glyphs (MENDING, HONING, BARBED, CIPHER, WITHERING, MIRING) can now be drafted into a sim deck and tournament-tested; previously canon-text-only.
  - **New engine primitives, mirrored across both `engine.py`'s `Duel` and `team_engine.py`'s `Battle`.** `self.objects = []` — a plain list of `{'kind', 'owner', 'position'}` dicts, engine-level, not tied to any Combatant's own state, since an Object outlasts whoever created it. `create_object(owner, kind)` appends one at the owner's current position, no unique ID needed since destruction removes the exact dict by identity. `attack_object(attacker, card, obj)` — the "attack a glyph instead of a combatant" mechanism from the original design: never rolls for damage, never triggers the attacking card's own Effect, just destroys the Object outright and discards the card normally (`_discard_or_return`, so a `returns_to_hand=True` card like ROLLOUT still behaves correctly even when used to destroy an Object); Fortress intercepts first — if anyone on the Object's own side holds Fortress, the attack redirects into a genuine, full attack against them instead (real RPS/damage/Effect, the article itself, not a stand-in), consumes their Fortress, and the Object survives untouched. A new module-level `_object_tick(engine, who)` fires from both engines' `start_of_turn`, alongside the existing `_ongoing_support_tick`: whoever occupies an Object's position on their own turn gets its payoff for free, no roll, no contest — Mending heals 1, Honing grants `next_attack_bonus`, Barbed grants `thorns`, Withering grants `weak`, Miring applies a real Initiative Shift -1 (`engine.initiative_shift`). A new `'attack_object'` action-kind was added to both engines' `take_turn` dispatch, alongside the existing `'attack'` branch.

Hazard-type Objects (Withering/Miring) strike the owner's enemies only, per the canon text's own "enemy" wording. (2) No AI policy can currently choose to call `attack_object` — it's a real, fully-working, directly-callable mechanism, but zero decision logic was added to any existing policy (same "no policy decision infrastructure yet" scope limit this whole session kept hitting for Wait/tactical positioning).   - **A genuine, flagged Ward exception, not a quiet reuse.** Ward's own rule (`rules/card-glossary.md`, Debuff) only ever covers what an *enemy* applies to you — this is the first card in the game where Ward blocks something a combatant inflicts on **themselves**. Implemented by calling the existing `warded()` helper directly on the self-Blind specifically — the Frontline enemies' Blind is unconditional and lands regardless of whether the caster's own Ward fires. Called out explicitly in both the card text ("Ward may prevent your own Blind") and the `content.py` comment, not left implicit.
  - **Verified directly, not just reasoned about**: a clean win applies Blind to every Frontline enemy and the caster, sparing Backline enemies entirely; a tie whiffs the Effect completely (`me._tie` gate holds through the real `attack()` pipeline, not just the isolated function); a caster holding Ward has their own self-Blind blocked and Ward consumed, while the enemies' Frontline Blind still lands unaffected; the Defensive Bonus (unchanged, Attacker gains Blind) still fires normally. Then the standard 5,000-duel and 5,000-team-battle stress test with it live in every hand, zero crashes; full standing regression suite re-run, confirmed identical to baseline (expected — no roster deck runs it). Print sheet regenerated again (still 116/13, text-only change).
