- **A3 — a real, reusable engine mechanism: retaliation targeting for instinct-driven creatures, built to hit Drew's explicit design targets (80-85% chance of a collapse, ~5%/10%-red-zone chance of an actual death) for the Basin fight, tuned and tested live rather than guessed at.** Two of Drew's hunches tested directly, one confirmed, one reversed:
  - **"Every dice needs increased by 2" — tested and reversed, not confirmed.** Bumped only the boss pair's own card dice (+2 die-size steps, party unchanged) and re-ran the same 10,000-battle test: win rate rose to 9.3%, collapse rate to 84.6%, but death rate got *worse* (28.5% → 32.7%). Bigger single hits don't shorten the fight enough to matter; they just make any follow-up hit on an already-collapsed character more lethal. Real, tested counter-evidence against the old parked "+2 dice" instinct (`memory.md`'s own earlier entry on it) — at least for this specific problem; the global version of that idea is still untested and unrelated to this finding.
  - **"They'd focus heavily on whoever was hurting them" — tested and confirmed, the real lever.** Built `Combatant._last_attacked_by` (set in both engines' clean-win damage path — a real, if scoped, gap: it only covers the main attack-damage path, not every defensive-counter sub-case) and a new `me.retaliates` opt-in flag read by `TeamTactician._pick_target` (`team_policies.py`): an instinct-driven creature re-locks onto whoever last landed a real hit on it instead of re-rolling randomly, once its current target collapses. **A real regression caught and fixed before it shipped**: the first pass gated this on `me.mind <= 2` directly, which silently changed FOUR existing ROSTER archetypes' behavior (volk/adept/warden/vanguard all have Mind 2 for unrelated reasons) and broke the standing regression suite — caught by actually re-running it, not assumed clean. Fixed by switching to an explicit opt-in flag instead of inferring from a stat other things also happen to have; regression suite re-confirmed byte-for-byte identical to baseline after the fix.
  - **Results, same 10,000-battle test each time**: retaliation targeting alone: 8.3% win / 81.3% collapse / **13.8% death** (down from 28.5%, still just over the 10% red zone). Adding a second refinement — even the "no valid retaliation target yet" fallback prefers standing targets over Collapsed ones before falling back to the full pool — dropped death rate to **0.0%, confirmed at 30,000 battles**, while collapse rate held steady (81.1%) and win rate stayed reasonable (10.5%). Overshoots the ~5% target on the safe side rather than landing in it. **Left open, not decided**: which of the two variants (13.8% death/close-to-red-zone, or 0%/too-safe) to keep, or whether a middle ground is worth finding — Drew's call, not resolved in this entry.
  - **Side note, not built, just observed**: this mechanism substantially answers Drew's separate "should there be a hidden threat-level mechanic" question without needing one — once a creature targets whoever's actually attacking it, the party already has real, direct control over who gets hit, just by choosing who swings. No new system built for that; flagged as a likely-sufficient answer, not a closed decision.
- **Follow-up, same thread — "never" was too absolute, and the dice-bump idea got a second, fairer test.** Drew, on the 0% death result: real combat "manipulating perception" is the actual design direction he's circling (not a traditional threat-level number, which he called out as too predetermined for how real enemies fight) — logged as a real, distinct design idea below, not built. Concretely: "never targeting a collapsed player is too much. 5% chance." Made the exclusion probabilistic instead of absolute — `me._collapse_target_chance` (default 0.05, tunable per-combatant, not a global constant) — then swept the actual value empirically rather than assuming 5% meant 5%: at the literal 5% per-event chance, battle-level death rate was only 0.63% (most fights don't have many re-target events, and a picked target still has to actually get hit hard enough to cross the death floor). Swept 0.05/0.15/0.25/0.35/0.50 directly: **0.35 lands at 5.17% battle-level death, 81.2% collapse, 10.3% win** — hits Drew's stated target almost exactly. Then re-tested the parked "+2 dice" idea a second time, this time as "+4" per his own suggestion ("maybe even a d4 increase... that's a chance at a 2 shot") and with the NEW tuned targeting logic active rather than the old random one: boss-only dice bumped 4 steps (d2→d6, d4→d8), collapse-chance re-swept for the new context, landed at 0.25: **win 15.9%, collapse 85.7%, death 4.67%** — hits the death target just as well, but collapse rate sits at the top edge of the 80-85% band rather than centered, since collapse rate tracks raw damage more than the targeting-chance parameter. Two real, tested candidates left for Drew to pick between (baseline dice/centered collapse, or +4 dice/punchier-but-edge-of-band) — not resolved in this entry. Scope note carried forward: still boss-kit-only, not a test of the old global "+2 to every card" idea.
- **A3 — the biggest single mechanical change of the whole session: a global +2-sides dice step-up on every attack card, plus a full rewrite of Collapse & Death, both engine-wide.** Drew committed to what had been parked since much earlier this session, plus a genuinely new death mechanic. Full trail, since the design got refined twice before landing:
  - **Global dice step-up, confirmed as a full die-size shift** (not an additive +d2 roll, which I misread on the first pass and Drew corrected: "no a step up. +2 to the number of sides"): d2→d4, d4→d6, d6→d8, d8→d10, capped at d10 (no d12 introduced — my own scoping call, flagged, since the physical dice set this game uses tops out at d10 and SACRIFICE STRIKE was already there). Applied to every `add()` registration in `content.py` (112 cards) via a verified regex pass, **plus every custom `damage=` function's own hardcoded die literal** (16 cards — BURN BRIGHT, FRACTURE, TRACE, TWIN STRIKE, GAMBLER'S RUIN, PRESS THE INJURY, PATIENCE, UNDERSTANDING, AFTERIMAGE, DRAG, COIL LATCH, STILL GROUND, ROLLOUT, GORE, EXPOSED, plus five Counter-Attack instances in SACRIFICE STRIKE/DEFLECT/BREAK/CONSUME/SEISMIC REDIRECT's own defenses that mirror each card's own base die), checked individually rather than blanket-regexed — a real risk flagged and avoided: Blind/Evade's `roll(2,...)==1` coin-flips and the 1d6+Soul initiative roll are NOT attack-damage dice and would have silently broken if a naive sweep had touched them. Caught two of my own misses mid-pass (UNDERSTANDING's no-hand fallback branch, CONSUME's defense) by re-grepping every `roll(`/`_rolled_die(` site in `content.py` after the first pass and checking each result against the intended mapping, not just trusting the initial edit.
  - **Deadly/Weak's own bonus die bumped too** — Drew: "deadly can be add 1d6" (was d4). Weak moved with it, d4→d6, for the same reason the two were made symmetric in the first place; flagged as my own inference (only Deadly was explicitly confirmed) rather than silently assumed.
  - **Death rules fully rewritten — the actual mechanism, nailed down after two rounds of correction.** First draft (mine) had the collapsing hit count as "hit 1 of 2." Drew's correction: **"no they drop to zero from a hit. then from there it takes 2 hits to kill, unless the first of those two hits is equal to or greater than the targets max HP. in that case it only takes 1 hit on someone with 0."** Final mechanism: the hit that collapses a standing combatant is free, never counted; from 0 HP, it then takes exactly 2 more genuine attack hits to kill, UNLESS the *first* of those two alone deals damage ≥ max HP (instant kill, no second hit needed) — the second hit, whatever its own size, is unconditionally lethal once reached. HP no longer goes negative at all, ever (removed the old `death_floor()` = `-ceil(maxHP/2)` mechanic entirely, along with its own now-dead method). Built as a new Combatant field (`_hits_while_collapsed`, reset on revival) and a shared helper (`_apply_collapse_death_check`, `engine.py`, called from both engines' genuine-attack resolution only — deliberately not from generic `deal()`, since Unpreventable/Thorns/status damage must NOT contribute to the 2-hit tally). That last exclusion is the real mechanism behind Drew's own acknowledged tradeoff, "this does have a ripple effect of weakening unpreventable damage slightly" — those sources can still hurt a Collapsed character, they just can no longer kill one on their own, which the old magnitude-based floor sometimes let them do.
  - **Tested thoroughly before trusting either change**: 7 direct scripted tests for the death mechanism alone (collapsing hit is free, first post-collapse hit under max-HP doesn't kill, second post-collapse hit of any size kills, first-hit one-shot at ≥max-HP, Unpreventable damage never contributes, revival resets the counter, HP never goes negative), confirmed identically in both `Duel` and `Battle`. Then an 8,000-duel and 8,000-team-battle stress test with both changes live, zero crashes. Full standing regression suite re-run — **real, expected drift this time, not a bug**: average turn counts dropped noticeably (e.g. frost/steele 4.6→3.7 turns), matching the actual design goal of faster combat; death rates shifted variably across matchups (some up, some down) as the shorter-fights-plus-harder-to-die effects interact, which is the intended combined behavior, not a regression to chase back to baseline.
  - **Propagated immediately**: `rules/card-glossary.md`'s Deadly/Weak text, `CLAUDE.md`'s Stat Quick Reference table (Blue d4→d6, Red d6→d8, Green d2→d4, with a dated note). **Deliberately NOT propagated this pass**: the actual printed dice on all 240+ `cards/*.md` files, or print-sheet regeneration — flagged as a new, real Pending Propagation item above rather than attempted under time pressure at this scale.
  - **Then the actual test this was all in service of**: Garnet, Moss, Crimson, and Sky (the live 2v2 test's four characters, `playtesting/live-test-2v2.md`) against the CTR-12 mated-pair Stonecoils, all of today's changes live. Checked `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` for a duplicate copy of this same tier list — none found, nothing else to propagate for this specific reference.

Spot-checked several results directly against the sim's own `card.base_die` afterward (STRIKE/SACRIFICE STRIKE=10, GUARD=4, FLOW=6, GORE=8) — all matched exactly.

Bumped on the mapping's own logic, not sim-verified, since there was nothing to verify against.

Print sheets regenerated (`card-print-core.html` 116/13 pages, `-briarwatch.html` 20/3, `-mason.html` 6/1, unchanged page counts confirming no content was dropped); `items`/`items-field` sheets correctly untouched, since items aren't cards with an Attack die. - **A2 — a broken Mason support pillar added to the Shifting Burrow sinkhole, tying the Well Property collapse to the Masons' already-established underground work, plus a small environmental detail (standing water in the tunnels) layered on top of the live-narration walkthrough.** Drew, mid-narration: "right we should add a standing mason support piller right in the center of the sinkhole, the top broken off when the sinkhole fell in, the broken pillar is only visible from the outside with a high roll. a few inches of water in the tunnels, not in the burrower home, the basin, or the masons chamber."

What it actually is stays GM-only. A new `## GM Secret — Not Player-Facing` section (matching the established header convention from `locations/briarwatch.md`/`quests/the-larder-fence.md`) ties it directly to real standing lore rather than inventing a new one: the same underground stabilization project that already built the reinforced Living Chamber behind the Heavy Door (`quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`, Transition Zone — discovered via Masaharu's stress-line-marks check during the live test), which itself sits against the Masons' already-canon watchtower-and-fence line at Briarwatch's edge (`factions/the-masons.md`, Known Works). The pillar's failure — driven by the same seepage/pressure already in the Causality Chain, not a new cause — is named as the actual missing piece behind the sinkhole, with the Masons not yet aware. `factions/the-masons.md`'s Briarwatch-line entry extended with one sentence pointing to both quest files, keeping the fact recorded once and cross-referenced rather than duplicated.
  - **The water** — a new paragraph in `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md` right after the Causality Chain: a few inches of standing water through most connecting tunnels, with three explicit dry exceptions named exactly as given (the Borrower Settlement, the Rodent Burrow Basin, the Living Chamber behind the Heavy Door). Checked against the Living Chamber's pre-existing "dry" description before adding — no contradiction; the new note gives that existing fact a causal reason (reinforced Mason construction) rather than restating it.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding/GM-secret text, no `combatsimulations/` changes.
- **A2 — a Stonecoil egg in the Rodent Burrow Basin nest, and a traceable underground water current threaded through three already-mapped chambers, both continuing the live GM narration.** Drew: a single retrievable egg after the mated-pair fight (the pair's own reason for having grown bold enough to reach farmland at all); water that runs at the party's feet, away from its real source, as an environmental clue toward needing Borrower help on a water diversion — plus a direct question, "water is flowing from which unused chamber?"
  - **Identified from already-standing text, not invented fresh** — `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s own Deep Stream Channel entry already read "water here originates deeper, not from irrigation," which is exactly the deep, non-irrigation source Drew described; tagged it explicitly as the origin rather than adding a new room. Traced the path through existing geography with zero contradictions: Flood Shelf Chamber's two exits were already named (narrow predator tunnel / wider Borrower tunnel) but never assigned separate identities — now the predator tunnel is where the Deep Stream's current actually surfaces, and the Borrower tunnel is confirmed as the Split Seam, letting the same current climb on toward the Transition Zone. Flood Shelf Chamber is also tagged in its own header as *the* sinkhole chamber, since the Causality Chain already said "sinkhole formed above Flood Shelf Chamber" — a naming fix, not a new fact.
  - **Resolved Drew's own parenthetical uncertainty** ("I thought this was the ambush corridor honestly") — the tight space Steele and Ryder crossed with a dormant Stonecoil is the Split Seam, not the Predator Ambush Corridor (a separate, deeper, still-unused room gated behind Confined rules of its own). Split Seam's description now carries that callback directly, plus a note that it carries an actual moving current — distinct from the ambient standing water already logged everywhere else in the tunnels (last entry, above) — flowing away from Flood Shelf Chamber toward the Transition Zone.
  - **The well chamber's own end of the flow** — a new gap at the base of Broken Stair Chamber's far wall (Transition Zone), where the current drains onward past the mapped dungeon rather than pooling; ties back into why that chamber stays one of the three named-dry exceptions from last entry, now with a mechanism instead of just a stated fact.
  - **The egg** — added directly to the Rodent Burrow Basin entry, retrievable after the fight, framed as the in-fiction reason the pair pushed this far up into farmland territory rather than staying at normal depth.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding/environmental-clue text, no `combatsimulations/` changes.
- **A2 — Rodent Burrow Basin renamed Mated Stonecoil Nest; Flood Shelf Chamber expanded from 2 exits to the 4 it always should have had; Borrower Settlement given old, recovered flood damage.** Continuing the live GM narration, mid-scene, right after the party won the Nest fight. Drew caught a real geography gap by just asking the honest question: "there is only one way back out right? no other paths lead into the nest? if so that's wrong... back out into the sinkhole room there should be a total of 4 paths. 1 into the nest, 1 to the burrower home, one leading to the broken stair chamber, and the only one left to explore where the water is coming from."
  - **The rename** — "Rodent Burrow Basin" → "Mated Stonecoil Nest," since the mated pair (not the drowned-rodent flavor the old name was built around) is what the room's actually about now. Propagated everywhere the old name appeared in live canon: the Causality Chain's dry-rooms list, the Tactical Map Summary table, and `bestiary/stonecoil.md`'s cross-reference. Old name kept as a parenthetical note on the room's own header rather than silently vanishing, matching how renames get handled elsewhere in this repo (e.g. TELLS→RHYTHM BREAK earlier this session).
  - **Flood Shelf Chamber's geography, actually fixed rather than just re-described.** Last entry had already assigned identities to its two long-standing "narrow predator tunnel / wider Borrower tunnel" exits (water-source path and Split-Seam-to-Broken-Stair-Chamber path); Drew's question exposed that two more paths existed in play but were never written down — a tunnel to the Borrower Settlement and a tunnel down into the Nest. All four now listed explicitly, with the water-source predator tunnel confirmed as the one path still unexplored — this is a hub chamber, not a corridor with two doors, and the text now says so.
  - **The Nest itself confirmed single-entrance, on purpose** — one tunnel back to Flood Shelf Chamber, nothing else connecting there; Drew's worry was about the *sinkhole room's* connectivity, not the Nest's, and the Nest reading as a true dead-end (in the "no way forward except back" sense, not a design flaw) is correct and now stated plainly instead of left ambiguous.
  - **Borrower Settlement's own flood damage** — Drew: the Settlement should show real damage from the flood, but it's been long enough that the village has mostly recovered by the time the party arrives. Added a waterline stain and old fungal-mat scarring as physical evidence, explicitly framed as past/healed rather than an active crisis — checked against the Settlement's own "stays dry regardless" line in the Causality Chain (no contradiction: that line is about current ambient seepage, this is a one-time historical event the Settlement has since drained and rebuilt around).
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding/geography text, no `combatsimulations/` changes.
- **A2 — a retroactive Body check for the Broken Stair Chamber's own descent, plus a new group check for herding the rescued Fenwool back up the same stairs, caught by Drew mid-narration.** "Shoot. the broken stairwell. it needs a DC check for the party the first time they descend. makes sense for why I didn't, I fastened a rope to help balance my self while walking down the broken stairs." A real, honest gap: the existing Body (Easy) "climb broken stair" check (already in the Watchtower Ruins Checks list) was never explicit that it covers the chamber's own descent regardless of which of the two mirrored mouths gets used — the live party's route through Shifting Burrow never triggered it, and Drew's own in-fiction explanation (a rope anchored on the way down) is now the standing canon reason why, not a retcon that quietly erases the gap.
  - **Fixed, not duplicated**: `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s Broken Stair Chamber section now states plainly that the same Easy Body check applies to the first unaided descent from either mouth, and that a fixed rope (present now, since the party placed one) removes it entirely for anyone using the rope afterward.
  - **The new mechanic, Drew's own design, built as stated**: livestock can't hold a rope, so getting the Fenwool back up the same stairs needed its own check — "a group roll, take the avg. medium roll, if they roll low a fenwool falls and dies. the rest always make it." Built as a single group Body check, Standard (DC 13), averaging every player's roll: clears the DC, the whole herd makes it; misses it, exactly one Fenwool falls, capped there regardless of how badly the roll missed — no cascade, matching his exact wording. First "group check, take the average" mechanic in this file; not promoted to a reusable core-rules pattern, scoped to this one encounter beat.
  - **Also resolved, same exchange**: a real standing ambiguity in the dungeon's connectivity — Undercut Pillar and Unstable Cave (Mid Depth) had never been wired to any specific path before. Drew's own question ("next is the undercut pillar right? or is it the room with the pressure test first?") confirmed the natural reading: they sit along the same "follow the water" route the party is now on (the narrow predator tunnel out of Flood Shelf Chamber), in the order the file already lists them — Undercut Pillar (its own "water drip from above seam" line ties directly into the current being traced) then Unstable Cave, then on to Predator Ambush Corridor and Deep Stream Channel in Lower Depth. Confirmed in chat rather than written as a new doc section yet — worth capturing in the file itself next time this area gets a real edit pass, not urgent enough to interrupt live play for.
