- **A2 — water depth along the "follow the source" route made progressive rather than flat, a real physical-consistency catch from Drew mid-narration.** My own live description at Undercut Pillar had tied the room's ceiling drip directly to "the same current you've been following," implying one unchanging trickle. Drew: "does it really make sense to attribute that trickle to the flowing water at their feet? remember the deeper the party goes the deeper the water gets. in the sinkhole room it's a couple inches. here in the undercut pillar room it should be an additional couple inches, with it progressively getting deeper." Correct on both counts, and fixed in `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`: the Causality Chain's water paragraph now states the depth trend explicitly (shallow at Flood Shelf, deepening through Undercut Pillar and Unstable Cave, deepest toward Deep Stream Channel itself) and draws the actual distinction Drew's question was pointing at — a given ceiling drip (Undercut Pillar's own cracked seam) is a local symptom of the same hydrostatic pressure, not literally the single traced current; the real tell for a party following the water is the rising depth underfoot, room by room. Undercut Pillar and Unstable Cave's own entries each got a one-line depth bump matching the trend.
  - Not sim-related.
- **A2 — a real GMing mistake caught live, generalized into `rules/gm-guide.md`'s standing "When to Call for Rolls" guidance.** In the Unstable Cave, Rasp moved toward the rubble pile; I assumed the escalated action (digging) and immediately narrated the room's full Pressure Die mechanism — the DC, the 3-successes-to-clear count, the ceiling-drop trigger — before any roll had happened at all. Drew: "whoa slow down. that kind of info should be gated behind an easy perception roll. Rasp was actually curious about the top of the pile, is there a gap there?" Two separate errors in one move: assumed which action a player was taking instead of asking, and handed over mechanical thresholds for free instead of making them something a roll reveals. The room's own existing table already modeled the fix (`Inspect ceiling | No | Observe/Sense check reveals crack severity` sits right next to `Dig through rubble | Yes | 3 successes`) — I just didn't apply it to the rubble pile itself before jumping to the loud option. New paragraph added to `rules/gm-guide.md` right after the existing "roleplay should move the difficulty" guidance: mechanical detail gets earned by a roll, not handed over before one; a quiet, free, Easy-Perception look comes before naming a hazard's trigger conditions, and a room can (and often should) define both a loud action that trips something and a quiet way to learn about it first. Not sim-related.
- **A2 — the Fenwool herding roll resolved (14 average, clears Standard DC 13): the whole herd makes it up the Broken Stair safely, no losses.** Party then confirmed continuing past Undercut Pillar into Unstable Cave. Drew, on scope: the party could stop here, reinforce the pillar, and end the quest having only saved the Fenwool — a real, valid outcome, not a failure state — but asked to keep building out the rest of the dungeon (Unstable Cave, Deep Stream Channel, Old Flow Channel) regardless of where this specific live playthrough actually stops, since the content should exist either way. Not yet built past this point as of this entry — narration continuing live, canon text for Unstable Cave/Deep Stream Channel/Old Flow Channel already exists from before this session and doesn't need new construction unless play surfaces a gap, same as everywhere else visited so far.

- **A2 — a crit rolled live in the Unstable Cave, first assigned to Luminova Leaves, reverted and replaced once Drew asked for something more fitting.** Rasp's Easy Mind (Observe) check on the rubble pile's top succeeded (Drew's own math: "10 + mind" clears DC 11), spotting a gap instead of needing the full "3 successes to clear" brute-force dig — the smart-play discount playing out exactly as `rules/gm-guide.md`'s roleplay/difficulty guidance intends. Steele's follow-up Standard Body (DC 13) check to clear the gap wide enough to squeeze through crit ("double 10!!"), and the crit needed a find. First pass gave Luminova Leaves a Source line tying it to this exact debris fall — Drew: "no something more fitting," reverted cleanly (Canon Gate reversal in practice, same session it shipped). Replaced below.
- **A2 ⚑ new ecology, a real redefinition-test catch before shipping** — the actual find is an Emerald Frog, Drew's own description: "prized as much as a real gem... used not only in culinary dishes but also worn in jewelry... an extremely rare find down in the burrows (one of their natural habitats even though they are going extinct)." `bestiary/emerald-frog.md` already existed, and its established framing doesn't match that description at all — a common woodland frog, harvested in volume by hand for Senshi's restaurant dish, explicitly *less* common (not rare or endangered) near Briarwatch specifically because the limestone soil drains too fast. Read as a real conflict before writing anything: does "extremely rare, going extinct, gem-jewelry-grade" contradict "common, harvested in volume"? Resolved as a non-contradictory extension rather than a redefinition — added a **Burrow Population** section describing a separate, far rarer regional population living in the Hollow's own burrow networks (Borrower territory, not Senshi's supply chain), with deeper, truer gem-coloring than the common woodland variety, prized for both culinary and jewelry use, and going extinct — distinct from the common population the existing file already describes, not a rewrite of it. Cross-referenced both directions (`bestiary/emerald-frog.md` ↔ `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`, Unstable Cave). Flagged here rather than shipped silently, since Authority 2 extensions specifically may not redirect an existing theme — Drew's review is veto-after if this reads as changing the common frog's established rarity rather than sitting alongside it. Not sim-related — pure fauna/lore entry, no stat block (matches Fenwool's precedent: not every creature is a combat encounter).

- **A3 — Perception Modes renamed, Drew's explicit direction, repo-wide sweep.** Same live-play thread as the earlier Sense→Senses flag: Drew caught the matching problem on Mind's mode. "same issue with mind's perception being called observe, that infers eyes which infers body. mind is reasoning, body is senses, and soul is instinct. that's the sharp line. please update it." **Observe → Reason** (Mind), **Sense → Senses** (Body), Read (Soul) unchanged — Constitutional, and directly authorized rather than inferred. The canonical detailed definitions in `rules/resolution.md` (headers, the "One Table Rule" mapping) already fit the corrected framing without needing to change any actual mechanical meaning — Mind's mode was always "notice information that must be interpreted" (reasoning, not eyesight specifically), Body's was always "signals the body reacts to faster than thought" (the physical senses). This was a label fix, not a mechanics change.
  - **Scope counted before sweeping, same discipline as the earlier dice propagation**: found via `grep -rn "\bObserve\b\|\bSense\b"` across every `.md` file. Fixed in `rules/core-rules.md` (table + a dated footnote explaining the rename), `rules/resolution.md` (the real canonical section — three headers, the One Table Rule mapping), `rules/combat.md`, `CLAUDE.md`'s Stat Quick Reference, `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md`, and every live quest/bestiary/item file that named a mode: `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`, `quests/shifting-burrow.md`, `quests/turnroot-weald-adventure.md`, `quests/sour-tomatoes.md`, `quests/the-wallows-descent.md`, `locations/fog-basin.md`, `locations/abyssal-trench.md`, `bestiary/death-ball-sponge.md`, `bestiary/fogcaller.md`, `bestiary/echo.md`, `bestiary/flapjack-octopus.md`, `bestiary/rootstalker.md`, `bestiary/phase-leach.md`, `bestiary/future-lock-wasp.md`, `bestiary/the-descended.md`, `bestiary/elder-tower-creature.md`, `items/turnroot-weald-items.md`, `cards/flapjack-octopus.md`, `experimental/the-man-who-buys-injuries.md`.
  - **Deliberately left alone**: `bestiary/the-descended.md`'s **Vibration Sense** passive — an ability name, not a Perception Mode invocation, checked by reading the actual passive text before touching it rather than pattern-matching on the word alone. `archives/where-the-tracks-stop.md` and `archives/wall-reader.md` — genuinely frozen content, same convention as every other archives/ sweep this session (only `experimental/archives/` counts as an active parking lot). `memory.md`'s own historical log entries — untouched, per the standing rule that history doesn't get rewritten after the fact.
  - **Re-verified whole-repo after**: re-grepped for any remaining bare `Observe`/`Sense` outside `memory.md` and the two frozen archive files — zero matches.
- **A2 — Deep Stream Channel rebuilt as a real, hard cutoff point, not just the water's source.** Drew: 10 ft deep, a hard swim, "lots of hints about the dangers here because this is the real cutoff for exploration." Added to `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`: a Hard (DC 16) Body (Senses) check to cross against the current, generous environmental warning (cold, displaced water, even rhythm to floating debris, zero Stonecoil sign anywhere near the edge — the absence itself is the tell), and the actual fork: go back and solve it with the Borrowers (Mediation Arc, already established as the "right" path — they already know how to redirect water), or force the crossing and risk the Greater Stonecoil directly. Added a third, explicit Release Condition to the Greater Stonecoil's own entry (Tertiary — forcing this specific chokepoint) alongside the two already documented (Pressure 4, abrupt diversion), so the new trigger is real, not just narrated in one section and absent from the creature's own rules. **Explicitly not balanced as an encounter yet** — Drew's own call: "we can balance that fight later or I can even do it on the fly if the party really forces it." No stat block added or changed for the Greater Stonecoil this pass.

- **A1 — Mediation Arc given the Outcome line it was missing, plus a live playthrough proving it out.** `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s Player Pathways already gave Surgical Intervention and Escalation their own Outcome lines; Mediation Arc had Requires but no Outcome — a real doc gap, found by actually resolving it in play rather than caught on a read-through. Filled in matching style: sinkhole stabilizes, Pressure drops, Borrowers and Briarwatch reach real terms — the only arc where both sides come out ahead, and a note that the engineering idea still has to be proposed and understood (a Soul check carries the communication), not just declared.
  - **Proved out live**: Ryder gestured the rubble-pile-as-dam idea at the Borrower Settlement's own map, Soul (the mode formerly Read, now still Read — Body/Mind got renamed, Soul didn't) check, roll of 20 (15 + Soul 5) — clears Extreme (DC 19) outright. Elder agreed immediately; construction begins as a background epilogue, not a further combat/skill sequence. This specific live playthrough's Hollow Below Briarwatch closes via the Mediation Arc — the "best" ending the doc already described, now confirmed to actually play the way it reads on paper.
  - **Deliberately left open, on purpose**: the Old Flow Channel, the far bank of Deep Stream Channel, and the Greater Stonecoil were never engaged this playthrough — the party chose to solve the water problem rather than force the chokepoint (Deep Stream Channel's Tertiary release condition, added last entry, never triggered). Consistent with "the open questions are on purpose" — nothing forces that content to resolve just because this session did.

- **A2 — the Larder Fence scaled up for real (100 yards from the watchtower ruins, 20 ft posts, cable instead of wire), plus a new underground quarry tying the fence and the sinkhole pillar to one shared source.** Drew, in one pass: placement close enough to see from the ruins, post height, and a material upgrade — "the wire between the posts, let's upgrade that to thick cables." All three landed in `quests/the-larder-fence.md`'s Setup: distance and height stated plainly, and every one of the file's 6 uses of "wire" as the terrain's own name swept to "cable" (checked each site rather than blind-replacing — Positioning, the Barbed rules, the Rushdown/Pull interaction, the larder itself all still read correctly with the new material). Tollbird's own perch reads better for it — a bird "two fenceposts up" was already implicitly high, now it's an explicit, imposing 20 ft.
  - **The Quarry Offshoot** — Drew: a room off the sealed Living Chamber where it's clear the Masons ran a small quarry, the source of both the underground support pillar and the fence posts above. Added to `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s Transition Zone: a real, physically discoverable room (tool-marks matching the Heavy Door's own craftsmanship, plainly a quarry to anyone who looks) whose *significance* — that it fed two separate Mason projects, one below ground and one above — stays GM knowledge, same "visible but not self-explaining" treatment as Masaharu's stress-line-marks reveal earlier this session. Cross-referenced from both directions: `factions/the-masons.md`'s Briarwatch-line entry and `quests/the-larder-fence.md`'s own GM Secret both now name the shared quarry instead of leaving the fence posts' origin unexplained.
  - **Borrower diet** — Drew asked what Borrowers actually eat; answered and shipped rather than left as chat-only flavor: `bestiary/borrower.md` gained a Diet paragraph built from what already existed (cave rodents, already-established protein source) plus reasonable, non-contradictory extensions (the fungal mats doing double duty as food, not just fuel; surface root vegetables working down through cracks from Briarwatch's own fields, a nice quiet trade the farmers don't know they're part of; blind fish from the deeper stream). Explicitly excluded the Emerald Frog's Hollow population from the everyday diet — it's the one thing established as rare/endangered (open thread, above), and making it a dietary staple would have quietly undercut that before the thread even gets revisited.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding across all four touched files.

- **A3 — the Emerald Frog thread resolved, Drew's own call, closed out of Active Pending Threads.** He came back to it directly: "perhaps the difference is just in the hardness of the body? these rare ones a solid like gems where the other forest type is soft like a normal frog. but even those shouldn't be labeled as common. the forest type is still rare, just not as rare as the cave type. the forest type is probably still endangered from over harvesting." This is Drew redefining the established fact himself (Constitutional, his call to make, not mine to infer) — resolves cleaner than my own first-pass guess (deeper coloring as the distinguishing trait) and gives the culinary/jewelry split a real physical reason instead of just degree: the Burrow population's body is genuinely solid and gem-hard, which is *why* it can be preserved and worn as jewelry without decaying like ordinary tissue — the forest frog's soft body never could be, no matter how prized. `bestiary/emerald-frog.md` rewritten: forest frog is now rare (not common) and explicitly endangered from Senshi's own standing demand — a real, causal tie to a fact that was already on the page (the dish "requires volume") rather than a new unrelated cause invented for it. Burrow population's write-up updated to lead with hardness, coloring dropped as the emphasized trait. Not sim-related.

- **A3 — the Coil's origin filled in, Drew's own history, resolving the flagged thread from earlier this session.** "the coil location was originally a river serpent much like the stonecoil. the 'coralcoil.' the banks of several rivers are coral reefs where the river is close to the unheld ocean. these coralcoil variants are the same size as regular stonecoils under normal conditions but they can grow many times that the same way stonecoils do. the ancient civilization that lived where the abyssal ruins are, had a coralcoil in containment and the people were feeding it. the goal was to make a living bioweapon to use against the people from ashfall." Constitutional-level cosmology, directly dictated — checked against everything already on the page rather than assumed compatible, and it holds together cleanly:
  - `locations/the-coil.md`'s own "What Happened To It" already said the Coil was near the Abyssal Ruins the first time the War Engine fired, and left "whatever it was before" deliberately unnamed. `locations/ashfall-wastes.md` already established the War Engine was "aimed outward at an enemy it did in fact defeat" — the Abyssal Ruins civilization. Drew's new history answers the exact question the old text left open (why was it there, what was it) without contradicting either side: the Ruins' civilization was losing that war and building their own answer the slow way — a captive Coralcoil, deliberately fed toward becoming a living weapon — when the Engine struck first and ended the question before their weapon was ready. Its containment didn't survive the strike any better than the civilization did, and it fled into the Unheld the same moment everything else there ended.
  - **Shipped across five files, cross-referenced in both directions rather than stated once and left orphaned**: `locations/the-coil.md` (the core rewrite — the Coralcoil identity and the containment/bioweapon history folded directly into the existing "What Happened To It" narration, plus two new Related Documents lines), `locations/abyssal-ruins.md` (a new paragraph giving its previously-thin "ancient civilization" a real, specific thread, and its own stale Related Documents line about the Coil sharpened to reflect what's now known), `bestiary/stonecoil.md` (a cross-reference note establishing Coralcoil as the same species logic in a different habitat — explicitly flagged as not needing its own stat block unless an ordinary, unaltered Coralcoil becomes its own encounter, per "don't invent mechanics in isolation"), `world/geography-overview.md` (a new line in Rivers and Lakes: coral-reef riverbanks where a river runs close to the coast — ordinary growth, not Unheld-touched, and the Coralcoil's native habitat).
  - **One small clarity fix caught while drafting, not shipped as written on the first pass**: an early draft used lowercase "unheld" as a pun right next to "the Unheld" as a proper noun in the same sentence (the Coil going free of its cage right before fleeing into the ocean) — clever but genuinely confusing next to a capitalized cosmological term this load-bearing. Reworded for clarity before shipping; CLAUDE.md's own "clarity over cleverness" line, applied to my own draft rather than just cited at others.
  - Not sim-related — pure cosmology/lore, no `combatsimulations/` changes.

- **A3 — new keyword Vulnerable built and wired into the sim, plus a real fix to a previously-flagged unruled tie-break.** Drew: "new keyword, vulnerable. receive 50% more damage from the next successful attack against you. rounded down. 1 stack of vulnerable and 1 stack of resist cancel out. 1 stack of weak and 1 stack of deadly also cancel out." Checked first whether Weak actually needed the "-1d6" update he separately asked for — it didn't; both `rules/card-glossary.md` and `engine.py`'s `_rolled_die` already had Weak at a flat -1d6, done earlier this session alongside Deadly's own bump. Confirmed via direct grep across the whole repo (only hits were historical `memory.md` log lines and GAMBLER'S RUIN's unrelated own "Add 1d4" mechanic, explicitly kept as its own identity per `archives/multi-agent-notes.md`) — nothing left stale.
  - **Vulnerable**: `Combatant.vulnerable = 0` (new field, `engine.py`, shared by both engines). Consumed in `deal()` (both `Duel.deal()` and `Battle.deal()`, mirrored): 1 stack applies +50% damage rounded down `(amount * 3) // 2` to the next successful attack, then consumes itself — same shape as Resist's existing halving, opposite direction. Gated on `not unpreventable`, same as Resist (Vulnerable only touches real attacks, not Thorns/status damage). Documented in `rules/card-glossary.md` as a Debuff (Ward can prevent it, same category as Blind/Rooted/stat reductions).
  - **The cancellation rule, both pairs**: Resist/Vulnerable and Deadly/Weak each cancel 1-for-1 on consumption rather than one silently winning. This directly resolves a real gap `_rolled_die`'s own docstring had flagged since the big dice-rewrite entry earlier this session: "Deadly takes priority if both are somehow held at once (not a ruled case, just a defensible tie-break)." Implemented as a check-before-either-applies at the top of `deal()` (Resist/Vulnerable) and `_rolled_die()` (Deadly/Weak) — deferred to consumption time rather than refactoring every individual `+= 1` grant site across `content.py`, since nothing reads these fields for any purpose (AI targeting, etc.) before they're consumed at damage-resolution time; checked this directly via grep before choosing the deferred approach over the more invasive one.
  - **Verified before trusting**: direct scripted tests against real `Combatant`/`Duel` objects — Vulnerable alone (+50% rounded down, consumed, tested at two different base amounts including a genuine rounding case), Resist alone (unchanged, half rounded down), Resist+Vulnerable together (full damage, both consumed, no modifier), Deadly+Weak together (plain roll, both consumed), Deadly alone (still applies). Full standing regression suite re-run after (frost/steele, mire/volk, sage/adept, tempo/warden duels; steele+sage+adept/frost×3, vanguard×3/mire×3, patient_host/steele+sage+adept team battles) — numbers match previously-logged baselines where recorded (frost/steele avg turns 3.7, matching the post-dice-bump figure already on record), no crashes, no drift, since no card yet grants Vulnerable and the Deadly/Weak cancellation only changes behavior in the narrow case both are held at once.
  - **Not yet built**: no card grants Vulnerable yet — pure keyword/mechanism, waiting on an actual card (likely from the Gambler archetype, below) to use it.
- **A2 ⚑ new archetype + three real cards — the Gambler, built from Drew's own brainstormed card ideas, fully specified through follow-up rather than guessed at.** Drew clarified all three concepts precisely before anything got built:
  - **WILD CARD** (`cards/green-soul.md`) — his own correction of my first read: not "reveal top card, gain X if Y," but the reveal *replaces the played card entirely* once the RPS outcome is locked in — "you reveal, then replace the card you just played for the attack with the card you revealed off the top of the deck... this happens before damage effects and defensive bonuses happen... both this new custom card and the card revealed from the top of the deck go to discard." Built as a shared helper, `_reveal_top_of_deck_swap` (`engine.py`), called from both engines at the two points an outcome is actually about to use the card — the uncontested-win branch and right after `rps()`/`_rps()` resolves for the contested attacker/tie/defender-win branches, PLUS a second call site right before the defender's own Defensive Bonus fires (tie and defender-win branches only) — a real design realization caught while building, not just Drew's literal words: the card is symmetric by nature (any card has both an Effect and a Defensive Bonus), so WILD CARD needs to swap from *whichever side's deck is actually resolving*, attacker or defender, never the opponent's, and never in the attacker-win branch where the defender's card never resolves at all (swapping there would burn a card off their deck for nothing — caught and avoided before it shipped). WILD CARD itself registers with no damage/effect/defense functions at all (Card class already no-ops all three when absent) — the only functions that ever actually run belong to whatever gets revealed. Verified directly: swap on an uncontested win (revealed card's own damage function fires, both cards land in discard), swap on a tie (revealed card's Effect fires), swap when WILD CARD is chosen as a *defense* card on both a tie and an outright defender win (revealed from the DEFENDER's own deck, its Defensive Bonus fires), confirmed the swap does NOT fire — no card burned — when the attacker wins outright against a defending WILD CARD, and the empty-deck-and-discard edge case (resolves as a harmless blank, no crash). Full regression suite + 5,000/5,000 duel/battle stress test re-run after each of the two rounds of changes — byte-identical to pre-existing baselines, since no ROSTER deck runs WILD CARD yet.
  - **TABLE STAKES** (`cards/red-body.md`) — Drew filled in the colors he'd trailed off on: "unpreventable 4, mind inflict stagger, and green heal yourself and allies 3 HP" (Red left as originally stated). Built as `_table_stakes_effect`/`_table_stakes_defense` in `content.py`: discards one random card from hand (any card, status cards included — unlike CONSUME's own random-destroy, which deliberately excludes them for a different reason, a distinction worth keeping straight rather than copying blindly), branches on its color, does nothing on a colorless discard or an empty hand. Defensive Bonus (Gain Resist) is my own filler, not Drew's — flagged, not silently assumed. Verified directly: Red/Blue/Green branches each tested with a controlled single-card hand, plus the empty-hand no-crash case.
  - **DOUBLE DOWN** (`cards/red-body.md`) — I flagged the scope risk and proposed parking it text-only ("genuinely invasive engine work... want me to actually build this, or park it as a strong idea for later and keep it text-only for now while we playtest the simpler cards first?"); Drew picked that option: "can we put it in the coreset and just leave a note to keep it out of the sim until it's been playtested?" *(Corrected 2026-07-23 — this line originally credited the deferral to Drew alone; see Standing Reasoning for the mis-check and the fix.)* Shipped as real canon text only (a full second RPS exchange on a clean win, Initiative Shift -1 to the attacker as the Defensive Bonus) — deliberately NOT registered in `content.py` or given any engine support, since a same-turn bonus attack chained off another attack is genuinely invasive (turn structure, hand economy, initiative bookkeeping all at once) and untested. Logged as a real Pending Propagation item, below, rather than silently absorbed as "someday."
  - **The archetype concept itself** — Drew: "gambler is a character archetype. we should have those saved somewhere. Steele fits gambler the best in the party, Lefty second. but no one in the party is purely one archetype." Added a new **Archetypes** section to `rules/character-creation.md` (the natural home — character-build philosophy, not a new mechanical system) rather than inventing a new file: defines an archetype as an emergent playstyle rather than a class, explicitly non-exhaustive, with the Gambler as its first entry and the real party read (Steele best fit, Lefty second, nobody pure) recorded alongside the cards that define it.

- **A real mistake, caught immediately and fixed: a duplicate archetype system, built without checking whether one already existed.** Drew: "the other archetypes are mirror, coward, parasite, and builder. most of the colorless cards got introduced when these archetypes were brought in." Went to record this and found `agent-tools/archetypes.md` already exists — a genuinely established, canonical "fourth kind of content" (its own header explicitly cites CLAUDE.md's Four Kinds of Canonical Content for why it needed this separate home) with all four of these archetypes already fully documented, plus eight more (Escalator, Opportunist, Collector, Judge, Teacher, Cultivator, Weaver, and the Gambler entry I'd only partially known about). Worse than just missing it: last entry's addition to `rules/character-creation.md` didn't just duplicate this — it directly contradicted the file's own standing principle, quoted in its own header: "at the table archetypes aren't revealed, they are naturally discovered" (Drew's own words, already on record there). Writing archetype names into a rules file players might actually read was a real error, not a style nitpick.
  - **Fixed**: reverted the `rules/character-creation.md` addition in full. Updated the correct file instead — `agent-tools/archetypes.md`'s existing Gambler entry now lists WILD CARD and TABLE STAKES as shipped, and DOUBLE DOWN as canon-text-only/sim-pending (matching the exact phrasing precedent already set by the Builder entry's Mason Glyphs note: "Combat-simulator support still pending"), plus the real party read (Steele best fit, Lefty second).
  - **Checked, not assumed**: the Mirror entry already correctly attributes AFTERIMAGE and FOLLOW-UP (2 of the file's 3 colorless cards) as archetype-sourced, confirming Drew's "most of the colorless cards" claim against the actual file rather than taking it on faith — nothing further needed there. BECOMING (the third colorless card) isn't tagged to any archetype in the file and I didn't force it into one without him saying so.
  - **The actual lesson**: check for an existing home before building a new one, especially for anything meta/process-shaped rather than in-world content — the same category of miss as the earlier Fenwool/Rodent-Basin-path gaps this session, just one layer higher (structure, not content).

- **A2 — the farmer given real, bounded knowledge of the Borrowers, fixing a gap in the Hollow's own opening hook.** Drew: "the farmer knows about the borrowers, how could he not? just glimpses of them in the ruins and rarely in the open fields. he says suspects they might be responsible for the collapse... he wouldn't know for sure, he has no interest in poking around down in the caves. that's why he sent for help." A real, honest miss — the existing Boxed Read-Aloud in `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md` had him mention the sinkhole and the Fenwool losses but nothing about the tunnel-dwellers he'd almost certainly have glimpsed over the years, living this close to the watchtower ruins. Added a second line of dialogue in his established voice (tired, matter-of-fact, not frightened) plus a short GM note making the actual epistemic boundary explicit: glimpses and suspicion, not confirmation — he's right to wonder (the Borrowers really did widen their tunnels, per the Causality Chain) but has no way to know if that's the real cause, and the party still has to find out. Checked against the already-corrected Fenwool dialogue from earlier this session (Steele's "found something down there that ate all your fenwool" line) for consistency — no conflict, since the farmer's new suspicion is about the *collapse*, not the livestock loss specifically.
- **A3 — world name corrected: Eclipsera → Eclipseria, full repo sweep, Drew's own explicit and doubly-emphasized call.** "when people in the world of Eclipseria (the i isn't accidental, it's much easier to say Eclipseria) hear about this fact..." Constitutional — the setting's own name — but directly dictated, not inferred. Scoped before touching anything, same discipline as every other repo-wide sweep this session: 98 occurrences of "Eclipsera" across 32 files via `grep -roi`. Excluded `memory.md` (historical log, never rewritten) and two frozen `archives/` files (`multi-agent-notes.md`, `key-design-decisions.md`, matching the standing convention that only `experimental/archives/` counts as an active parking lot) — 29 live files swept, 61 replacements, verified zero remaining bare "Eclipsera" outside the excluded set afterward. Also renamed the file itself, `world/eclipsera-overview.md` → `world/eclipseria-overview.md` (git mv, history preserved) — checked for any live file referencing the old filename by path first; only the same two frozen archive files did, left untouched.
- **A2 ⚑ new content — an alternate campaign opening, "Washed Ashore," built from Drew's own first-pass description.** "the new campaign isn't going to start with a ship ride into vultures nest. it's going to start with the party waking up washed ashore on the beaches of the unheld ocean... my first thought is to use the carrion guide as the first contact." New file, `quests/washed-ashore.md`: the party wakes at (−1, −11) on the established coordinate grid (`world/geography-overview.md`) with no memory of crossing the Unheld and — deliberately, permanently unexplained — none of the marks every other account of that crossing leaves behind (`world/the-unheld.md`); a faint trail north through open rolling fields and rocky outcroppings to the Roadhouse at (0, −7), geographically consistent with the existing grid without needing to invent new coordinates; Aege the Carrion Guide (`locations/vultures-nest.md`) met halfway, same established voice and behavior (watches feet, doesn't explain, routes travelers to Briarwatch) reused rather than a new NPC invented, her line ("New arrivals must earn their keep") and a wordless point toward Briarwatch before vanishing, exactly as Drew described. Cross-referenced in both directions — `locations/vultures-nest.md`'s own Aege entry now notes the second appearance explicitly ("not a second character"), `world/geography-overview.md` points to the new file from the Basin section. Flagged clearly as a first pass, not a finished module (Drew's own "my first thought is," not a locked plan) — what happens between the shoreline and the Roadhouse, and what "earning their keep" actually cashes out to, are both still open. The astonishment/disbelief world-reaction detail and the Corvel cross-reference (`locations/vultures-nest.md` — the one existing precedent, a single marked survivor) are both recorded as a long-term thread, explicitly flagged not to pay off early.

- **A3 ⚑ Aege given real cosmology — an Echo of the Seat of Death, not the overused Seat of Pathfinding.** Drew: "the carrion guide is an echo. seat of death? it fits and I'd like to avoid using the seat of pathfinding for everything. the death of the fenwool and the potential for the briarwatch situation to worsen is what draws her attention? and it explains why she seems to know more than she should about the party." Checked the actual framework before writing anything (`mythology/seats.md`, `world/seats-archons-echoes.md`) rather than assuming Echo/Archon were interchangeable — they aren't: an Archon *is* a Seat's full expression (the Night Ferryman, confirmed Archon of Navigation); an Echo is a lesser, living alignment with a domain, staged (Resonance/Alignment/Incarnation), that never holds the Seat itself. Confirmed Death's Seat is genuinely open for this — no bearer listed anywhere, explicitly noted "not publicly known" in the existing Archons file — so making Aege its Echo (Stage II, Alignment) is a clean fit, not a redefinition of anything already claimed.
  - **The causal chain, exactly as Drew framed it**: her attention is drawn to Briarwatch specifically (not just "west" in general) because her domain is concentrated there right now — the Fenwool already killed by the Stonecoils, plus the sinkhole's own escalating collapse risk (`quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s Pressure Track) as more potential ending stacked on top. She reads that concentration the same way the Night Ferryman reads the Coil's restlessness before it surfaces — a domain sense, not investigation — which is the actual mechanism behind her already-established "knows more than she should" quality.
  - **Kept her established personal motive fully separate, on purpose**: her own reason for watching the road at all — the Turnroot Weald, her family's letter — stays hers, untouched by the Echo explanation. Two real reasons, stated as two, not collapsed into one.
  - **Shipped in four places, cross-referenced**: `locations/vultures-nest.md` gets a full GM Secret section (the actual mechanism); `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` gets a new **Known Echoes** subsection (Aege is its first entry, mirroring the existing Known Archons list's structure); `mythology/seats.md`'s Death entry gets a one-line pointer so the two Seat-tracking files stay in sync; `quests/washed-ashore.md` updated to split its own "she doesn't explain how she knew" line into two honest halves — why Briarwatch is now answered, why *this specific party* still isn't, since the Echo explains the first and not the second, and conflating them would have quietly closed a mystery Drew never actually resolved.

- **A2 — Washed Ashore's Aege scene grounded, per Drew's direct note: "let's bring her down to earth more."** Reworked `quests/washed-ashore.md`'s "Meeting Aege" section start to finish. Old version: a mystical mid-trail encounter, one cryptic line, a wordless point, then vanishing. New version, exactly as Drew described: the party reaches the Roadhouse under their own power; Aege happens to be spending the night there too, mid-journey from Vulture's Nest to the Turnroot Weald on her own business, not stationed anywhere waiting for anyone. She doesn't hand over a sealed letter — she just tells the party what she'd otherwise only have written down, over an ordinary shared evening. Real, load-bearing difference from her Vulture's Nest telling of the same worry, flagged explicitly rather than smoothed over: there, she stays behind and sends the party in her place; here, she's already going herself, and mentions Briarwatch only because it's the more sensible first stop for a party with nothing to their name yet. Confirmed the expected shape Drew named — Shoreline → Roadhouse → Briarwatch → Turnroot Weald, following after her once the Hollow's done — and wrote it into GM Notes as the likely path, not a forced one.
  - **A real device swap worth being honest about**: the Sealed Letter + Carrion Feather (`locations/vultures-nest.md`, `items/briarwatch-items.md`) belongs to the other telling specifically, and doesn't fit this one's timing — flagged directly rather than quietly ported over or quietly dropped. The Carrion Feather's own Source line ("Given by Aege upon delivering the party to Briarwatch") doesn't have a clean trigger moment in this version at all; left open for whoever actually runs it rather than resolved on the spot.
  - **Cross-referenced from `locations/roadhouse.md`** — a new line under its existing Session 1 Routing section (which already covers the original Vulture's Nest-opening route through here) noting the alternate Washed Ashore path also passes through, plus a Related Documents entry back to the new quest file. Split GM-only "why Briarwatch really pulls her" (the Death-Echo explanation, unchanged from last entry) from the still-fully-open "how she recognized this specific party" mystery, both restated to fit the new, more grounded scene without quietly resolving either one further than Drew has.

- **A1 — a real geography contradiction caught by Drew, fixed everywhere it appeared.** "turnroot isn't 4 days west of briarwatch. it's on the border of briarwatch. that's why we put so much work into the larder fence section." Correct, and already fully established elsewhere — `locations/briarwatch.md` and `factions/the-masons.md` both describe the watchtower ruins and larder fence as existing specifically to hold Turnroot Weald's own encroachment back from Briarwatch's fields, and the historical Translation Principle incident logged earlier in this file was about the *eastern* edge being wrongly claimed, never about whether Turnroot borders Briarwatch at all (it does, confirmed, at the western edge). The Sealed Letter's own text just never got checked against that fact when it was written. Fixed in three places: `locations/vultures-nest.md`'s letter ("Four days west of Briarwatch" → "Past the edge of your own fields"), `memory.md`'s own Active Pending Threads hook description, and `quests/washed-ashore.md`'s spoken version of the same beat — which actually simplifies once fixed, since Aege's route from Vulture's Nest to Turnroot Weald already passes directly through Briarwatch (same road, same westward direction, Turnroot immediately past the fence line) rather than detouring to it. Left Aege's own separate "grew up four days west [of Vulture's Nest]" backstory line untouched — that one's anchored to a different, much longer distance on the coordinate grid and was never the contradiction.

- **A1 — the letter fix from last entry had its own ambiguity, caught immediately.** "past the edge of whose own fields the farmers? the players? Aege?" Fair — "your own fields" in a letter addressed to the party doesn't parse; the party doesn't own fields in Briarwatch. Fixed to name Briarwatch directly: "Past Briarwatch's own fields, there is a forest." `quests/washed-ashore.md`'s own version of this beat already said "Briarwatch's own western edge" explicitly and didn't need the same fix.

- **A2 — the Sealed Letter's real, structural problem, named directly by Drew rather than found through more polish: "the real issue is the letter hasn't decided yet if it's written for the players to read or if it's written like family contacting Aege for help."** Exactly right — the old text blended both voices at once: reported speech about what her family said ("My family sent word...") sitting next to direct address to the party ("You've shown me you move carefully... Find them"), signed "— Aege," as if she'd sat down and composed a briefing letter. That's a real character violation, not just an ambiguity: everything already established about her (doesn't talk about her family, doesn't explain herself, "what she says arrives flat") argues against her ever writing that much explanation down for strangers.
  - **The actual fix**: the letter the party reads is her family's own original letter to *her* — never opened, seal still intact when she hands it over, written in a completely different, warmer voice, addressed to Aege by name, signed only "— home." She says nothing else because there's nothing of hers in it to explain. This reframes something already true (she doesn't talk about her family) into the actual mechanism of the scene instead of just a trait mentioned elsewhere. New reading, not previously stated: she never opened it herself either — couldn't, handed the whole unread burden to people she'd decided to trust instead.
  - **A real consequence, caught and handled rather than left dangling**: with the geography exposition gone from the letter (a family writing home to their own daughter would never describe where home is), the party needs another way to place it. Added a short GM note: they don't need it spelled out, since they've spent the entire Hollow job standing next to the one forest it could possibly mean — Turnroot Weald, right at Briarwatch's fence line. The connection is there to make, not handed over — matches the same show-don't-explain register as everything else built around her and the Patient Host this session.
  - **`quests/washed-ashore.md` updated to match** — its own line about what Aege "would otherwise have only put in a letter" no longer fit a woman who doesn't write her own letters; reworded to make the actual contrast explicit: hides behind her family's unopened letter in one telling, has no letter to hide behind and just says it out loud in this one.

- **A1 — a contradiction in last entry's own fix, caught immediately: "why is it sealed? didn't Aege receive it in vultures nest, read it, and make her way to turnroot?"** Right, and a real miss — the existing "GM — Backstory" text already says "her family sent word asking for help. She won't go herself. She's been watching the road for the right party," which only makes sense if she'd already read it; "she never opened it herself" (my own addition, last entry) directly contradicted that. Fixed: she read it, same as the established backstory requires — what she hands the party is her own letter, resealed rather than freshly written, not one she left unread. Kept the part that actually solved the real problem (it's in the family's own words, not hers, so she never has to explain any of it out loud) and dropped the part that didn't hold up (that she'd never read her own mail).

- **A2 — Aege's arc simplified for real this time, root cause removed instead of patched again.** Drew: "letter is totally confused now. its because of the old framing of looking for the right party to give it to. drop that. she got the letter and is going herself to help that's all. she just so happens to run into the party at the roadhouse on her way to help her family. remove all your reasoning from it and change aege's document so that she's no longer seeking the party." Right diagnosis — every patch so far (sealed vs. resealed, whose voice, why she trusts this party) was propping up "she won't go herself, she's watching the road for the right party," a framing that was never load-bearing and kept generating new contradictions the more it got poked. Cut it instead of patching around it again.
  - **`locations/vultures-nest.md`**: backstory now reads plainly — family sent word, animals turned aggressive, she's going herself, Briarwatch's already on that road. The Sealed Letter section (the family's-voice-vs-her-voice fix, the resealing explanation, all of it) replaced by a much shorter **Telling the Party** section: she tells them plainly at the Roadhouse the first night out, asking nothing of them. Carrion Feather still passes at Briarwatch, unchanged, since she still personally guides them that far. GM Secret trimmed of its own restated justification paragraphs per "remove all your reasoning from it" — kept the facts (Echo of Death, Stage II, Briarwatch's domain concentration), cut the running commentary explaining why each fact was true.
  - **`quests/washed-ashore.md` brought into alignment rather than left as a second, different story**: both openings now agree on Aege's actual situation (got the letter, going herself, no one recruited or tested). The one real remaining difference, now stated plainly instead of hedged: at Vulture's Nest she's the party's actual guide and delivers them to Briarwatch before continuing alone; at the Roadhouse in Washed Ashore she's just a fellow traveler for one night and leaves ahead of them, so the Carrion Feather's usual trigger doesn't fire there — flagged once, cleanly, not treated as an unresolved mystery anymore.
  - **`memory.md`'s own Active Pending Threads entry updated to match** — the last version of this same entry still said "she can't go herself," already stale after this fix; corrected in place.

- **A1 — Aege given her own character file, matching the established split already used for Masaharu.** Drew: "Aege needs her own character file that only details who she is. so that it's not all housed in the vultures nest doc" — right call, unprompted by any contradiction this time, just overdue organization once she'd grown this much (appearance, voice, backstory, an Echo-of-Death GM Secret, two separate campaign appearances) while living entirely inside a location file. Checked the existing convention before inventing a new one: `characters/masaharu.md` already holds his character-intrinsic content (appearance, stat block, general behavior) while `locations/vultures-nest.md` keeps only what's specific to using him *there* (why he's in the Nest, the FourthEye plot hook). Applied the same split to Aege.
  - **New file, `characters/aege.md`**: appearance, voice, what she does, backstory, the GM Secret (Echo of Death), and a **Where She Appears** section covering both tellings side by side (Vulture's Nest guide role vs. Washed Ashore's one-night Roadhouse meeting) so the "same character, not a retcon" note lives in one place instead of being restated in both quest files.
  - **`locations/vultures-nest.md` trimmed**: her entry now covers only what's local to finding and using her at the Nest specifically — Finding Aege (the rumor/tracking mechanics), Soft Gate (the route-choice mechanic), and Telling the Party (still here since it's the next beat in this location's own Session 1 Routing sequence, even though the scene itself happens at the Roadhouse a day out). Backstory, voice, and the GM Secret all point to the new file instead of repeating it.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding organization, no `combatsimulations/` changes.

- **A1 — the letter's actual content fixed, third pass on this same problem, and this one's about the story not the structure.** "again the darn letter. I want it be right. the problem mentioned shouldn't be restless animals on the edge of turnroot. it should be restless roots deep in the forest." Not a voice or causality issue this time (those are both fixed) — the substance was wrong. Changed everywhere it appeared — `characters/aege.md`, `quests/washed-ashore.md`, `locations/vultures-nest.md`, and `memory.md`'s own Active Pending Threads entry — from "animals at the edges turned aggressive" to "something's gone wrong deep in the forest, not at its edges — the roots themselves have turned restless." Checked this against what's already established in `locations/turnroot-weald.md` ("roots surface like old roads, then sink again") and `bestiary/root-heart.md` (the deep-forest boss built entirely around disturbed, reaching roots) before writing it — genuinely resonant with existing lore, not an arbitrary reskin. Added one flagged, deliberately unresolved line to `characters/aege.md`: whether this is literally the Root Heart stirring is a live question, not asserted as fact.

- **A1 — Kess and Corvel given their own character files, per Drew's follow-up after Aege ("what other characters still need their own file I wonder").** Ran a real search (Explore agent) across every location/quest file rather than guessing — found both scattered across multiple files already with no single source of truth, exactly the Masaharu/Aege pattern. Also surfaced Lily, Hess & Cob, and Moth as strong secondary candidates, not yet built (Drew's call whether/when).
  - **`characters/kess.md`** (new): the Cartographer's granddaughter, the mine rescue, the traded memory, her tattoos, her relationship to Moth. **A real, previously one-directional gap closed while building this**: `characters/giblets.md`'s own Connections list never mentioned Kess at all, despite `characters/mortis.md` and `locations/underground-bazaar.md` both strongly implying Giblets was the "ex-adventurer with faulty memory" who escorted her into the mine (his own established repeated-resurrection memory gaps are the exact match for that description). Confirmed this is a safe connection to state plainly rather than a new invention — nothing it touches contradicts, and it explains an otherwise-unexplained detail (her tattoo reacting near his stall) — and added it to `characters/giblets.md`'s Connections section, plus fixed `characters/mortis.md`'s own stale Kess pointer (was aimed at `locations/underground-bazaar.md`, now points to her real file).
  - **`characters/corvel.md`** (new): full backstory (his own Unheld crossing 15 years ago) and the actual mechanism behind his stall (Pneum's ritual barges, disease-as-worship, his own sincere non-understanding of the problem) consolidated from two files that each only had half the picture.
  - **Corvel's duplication, the specific thing Drew asked about**: `locations/vultures-nest.md` had his personal backstory (the crossing, "connected to the People of Promise — the party won't know this yet") but never explained the mechanism; `locations/pneum.md` had the actual mechanism (the barges, the disease, the quarantine) under its own "Corvel — The Vulture's Nest Thread" header but never mentioned his personal history at all. Neither contradicted the other — they were just two halves of the same person, filed in two different location docs because each was written for what that specific location needed at the time. Consolidated into one file; both location docs trimmed to keep only what's local (the stall's presence at the Nest; the discovery/quarantine plot state at Pneum) and now point to `characters/corvel.md` for the rest.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding organization, no `combatsimulations/` changes.

- **A3 — new rules file, `rules/items.md`, giving item creation the same standing checklist cards already have.** Drew: "we need to work on making item creation a standard part of the design process... just as much as we make cards and build decks." Checked what actually existed first (`grep` across `rules/*.md`) rather than assuming a gap — found only a few terse lines in `rules/core-rules.md` and `rules/character-creation.md` (3 slots, only equipped items grant effects, Artifacts are Seat-aligned) and nothing like `rules/cards.md`'s die-tier design guidance for the other side of the game's whole item economy, despite every location already shipping its own items file.
  - **The clarifying rule Drew opened with, made explicit rather than left implicit**: fiction is unrestricted — a character can dress and carry however the story supports; only equipment with an *actual mechanical effect* competes for the three Equipment Slots. The slot system was never a cap on what you can wear, only on how much active mechanical power you can stack at once. This wasn't stated this plainly anywhere before.
  - **The Weapon/Armor tier system, exactly as specified**: three tiers, each a flat power budget (+1/+2/+3 attack damage for Weapons, −1/−2/−3 damage received for Armor) that can be spent whole on the raw number or split across smaller effects. Formalized the implicit conversion rate in Drew's own worked examples (tier-2 armor: −1 dmg + start with 1 Resist; tier-2 weapon: +1 dmg + Deadly at combat start; tier-1 weapon: Weak on first successful attack, whole budget on one debuff) into an explicit "1 point ≈ one flat damage/reduction, or one self-buff stack, or one enemy-debuff stack, usually gated to trigger once per combat" scale, with a note that spikier effects (Critical, Immunity, Fortress, full Ward) either eat a whole tier alone or need a real restriction to stay in budget.
  - **More fastball ideas, per Drew's ask, deliberately left unnamed**: a spread across both Weapon and Armor at all three tiers (Evade-on-first-hit, Scry-on-first-attack, Blind-on-clean-win, free Rushdown, three-way budget splits, and one whole-budget spike per tier — Critical for weapons, Ward or Fortress for armor, Immunity-on-Collapse) — genuinely just budget-legal shapes, not finished cards; they get identity when actually built as real equipment archetypes, matching how `agent-tools/archetypes.md` treats card archetypes the same way.
  - **Artifacts explicitly carved out of the tier system**, not forced into it — per their own established framing (`rules/character-creation.md`: "resonant... cosmological, narrative, and mechanical... a statement about what you're willing to be near"), they should stay bespoke and Seat-domain-specific, built one at a time like a signature card set rather than pulled from a shared budget table.
  - **Cross-referenced from both existing homes** (`rules/core-rules.md`, `rules/character-creation.md`) rather than left to be found only by people who already knew to look for it, matching how `rules/cards.md` is cross-referenced from the same two files for the card side of the same question.
  - Not sim-related — items have no combat-simulator representation, consistent with how consumables are already handled everywhere else in this repo.

- **A1 — real balance catch on `rules/items.md`'s own first example, fixed same day it shipped.** Drew: "fortress itself is one of the lowest value keywords when viewing it through an action economy lens, the party still takes the damage, no real advantage gained without pairing it with other things like resist or evade... it could even go on a tier 1." Correct — Fortress only redirects who eats a hit, it never actually prevents damage the way Resist/Evade do, so pricing "start combat with Fortress" as a whole Tier 3 (equal to a flat −3) badly overrated it. Fixed: removed it as a standalone Tier 3 entry, added a general pricing-caveat paragraph ("not every keyword is worth the same point regardless of context — check actual value, don't just price by name"), and replaced the example with Drew's own fix — Tier 1 armor: start combat with Evade 1 and Fortress 1, cheap enough to ride along with a real effect at the cheapest tier rather than ever anchoring one alone. Real lesson worth keeping: this doc's own tier-budget system is only as good as the per-keyword pricing feeding it, and that pricing needs actual play/design judgment, not just "sounds strong."

- **A3 — keyword renamed: Fortress → Protect, Drew's own naming call, full repo + sim sweep.** "fortress too strong a name? what's better that's not being used already? protect? actually I really like protect." Checked "Protect" wasn't already claimed as a keyword/card name anywhere first (only ordinary English usage in prose) before committing. Scoped the same way as every other rename this session: 14 live files (10 `.md`, 4 `.py`), 51 case-insensitive matches, swept via script — `Fortress`→`Protect`, `FORTRESS`→`PROTECT`, `_fortress`→`_protect` (the engine's own internal attribute name, both engines + `content.py` + `team_policies.py`). `memory.md`'s historical log and `archives/` left untouched, matching every prior sweep's convention.
  - **Two real bonus fixes found while in there, both flagged rather than silently folded in**: (1) `team_policies.py`'s AI value-heuristic had a genuinely dead reference, `"FORTRESS STANCE"`, a card name that was never actually registered in `content.py` — the real card that grants the keyword is `INTERCEPT` (`cards/red-body.md`), already correctly listed right next to the stale name in the same tuple. Fixed to just `INTERCEPT`, removing the phantom entry rather than renaming it to "PROTECT STANCE" and preserving a bug. (2) A blind rename briefly turned that fix into a duplicate `("SHARED BURDEN", "INTERCEPT", "INTERCEPT")` — caught immediately by rereading the line, fixed before it shipped.
  - **A separate, smaller dead-code oddity noticed but NOT touched, since it's out of scope for a naming request**: `content.py` has an orphaned `_protect_effect`/`_protect_defense` pair (renamed along with everything else, still functionally identical to before) that appears to have never been wired to any `add()` card registration — dead code predating this session, not something a keyword rename should silently clean up. Worth a look next time cards get a real review pass.
  - Full regression suite + direct `build_cards()` import check re-run after — byte-identical to every established baseline, confirming a pure rename with zero behavior change.

- **A1 — three quick follow-ups on the same items/Protect thread, all confirmed or fixed same session.** (1) Confirmed `rules/card-glossary.md` and every `cards/*.md` file were already caught in the Fortress→Protect sweep last entry — checked directly rather than assumed, all clean. (2) Added the Tier 3 stat-point option to `rules/items.md`, exactly as given: a Tier 3 Weapon or Armor may grant +1 to a single stat (Mind/Body/Soul) with nothing else attached, priced at the top tier since a stat point moves more than one thing (hand size, max HP, or the initiative roll) unlike a flat bonus. Added to both the main tier table and the Weapons/Armor fastball lists. (3) Removed the orphaned `_protect_effect`/`_protect_defense` pair from `content.py` outright — Drew: "the card isn't getting made" — rather than leaving dead code around because it happened to still work. Re-verified `build_cards()` imports clean and full regression suite unchanged after.
- **Items-in-the-world survey, Drew's direct ask, answered as research not a build.** "what items with mechanics do we have in the world currently... turnroot has luminova leaves sure but I know that's not it there. and the underground bazaar might have some." Real findings, not guessed: `items/turnroot-weald-items.md` is already the most fully-built items file in the repo (11 real entries — Echothorn Seed, Luminova Powder, Sap Vial, Vision Shard, Rusted Armor, Spider Silk Rope, Root Fibers, Rootstalker Core, Harvest Bead, plus two Senshi quest-ingredient items) and already has its own genuine mechanical **Luminova Powder** (ground Luminova Leaves, heal 4, sourced from "Luminova Clearing") — distinct from the plain **Luminova Leaves** (heal 2, no Source line at all) sitting in `items/hollow-and-weald-items.md`'s shared Hollow+Weald file. Drew's instinct was right: Luminova Leaves isn't cleanly "the" Turnroot item — it's an unsourced entry in a file straddling two locations, sitting right next to a very similar, better-sourced item that basically does the same thing at a different power level. Flagged, not resolved. **The Underground Bazaar has no defined mechanical items at all** — checked directly, all its item-adjacent content (Willem's paintings, the Soul Economy trades) is pure narrative, no `Use:`/`Effect:` text anywhere, and there's no `items/underground-bazaar-items.md` file. A real gap, not an oversight to fix uninvited. Also surfaced along the way: `locations/vultures-nest.md`'s "Harwick Sundries — Counter Items" (Tuning Fork Sword, Desire Compass, Guard's Boots, Ticking Box) are deliberately mechanics-free mysteries, not part of this inventory; and `items/fog-basin-items.md`'s Fog Goggles is the one existing item actually labeled "Artifact" already in play — worth checking against `rules/items.md`'s just-written Artifact framing (Seat-aligned, cosmological weight) next time that file gets real attention, since its current text doesn't obviously tie to a specific Seat.

- **A2 — items split into two files, per Drew's direct restructuring call, plus real gold pricing on Turnroot Weald's items.** "items.md is for containing items sorted by source location... move the weapon armor artifact stuff to a new equipment md. these will be important for quick reference when designing locations. who is trading with who, where does it make sense for items/equipment to show up." A real, correct distinction: what I'd built as `rules/items.md` last entry was a design GUIDE (how to build equipment), not a CATALOG (what already exists, and where) — two different documents with two different jobs, and Drew caught that they'd been merged into one.
  - **New `rules/equipment.md`**: everything that was in the old `rules/items.md` (the Dress However You Want principle, the Weapon/Armor tier budget, the fastball ideas, Artifacts) plus the new **gold-value table** — Tier 1 ≈ 200g, Tier 2 ≈ 800g, Tier 3 ≈ 2,000g, stated as a reference point GMs can flex for scarcity/condition/story, not a hard price-lock.
  - **Rewritten `rules/items.md`**: a real catalog, every item in the repo sorted by where it actually comes from — Briarwatch, the Hollow/Weald shared file, Turnroot Weald, Vulture's Nest, Fog Basin, Kaine's Lightning Loop, no-fixed-source consumables — plus a **Who Trades With Whom** section naming the real trade logic already implicit across the repo (Senshi buys Weald ingredients, Pell only ever charges in memories/secrets never coin, Borrowers trade goodwill not currency, Aege/the farmer hand items over as direct narrative rewards). Flagged rather than silently resolved: Harwick Sundries' mystery counter items are explicitly NOT part of this inventory (deliberately mechanics-free); the Underground Bazaar has zero mechanical items and would need its own pricing logic given its whole premise is trading in memory/debt, not coin; and — a genuinely new find while researching — **the Lightning Loop** (`items/lightning-loop.md`, Kaine's ring) is the one existing Artifact that actually matches the new Seat-alignment framing directly (Storm Seat), but "Storm" doesn't appear anywhere in `mythology/seats.md`'s own Seat list — a real gap, flagged, not fixed here.
  - **Turnroot Weald items priced directly at their source**, since Drew asked for a first pass: Echothorn Seed ~40g, Luminova Powder ~30g, Sap Vial ~60g, Vision Shard ~100g, Rusted Armor ~80g, Spider Silk Rope ~50g, Root Fibers ~15g/strand, Rootstalker Core ~250g, Harvest Bead ~150g, and the two Senshi quest-ingredients (~40g/~35g, each noting the condition that lowers or requires the price). Priced by relative rarity/power against each other and sanity-checked against the new equipment tiers (Rootstalker Core's "very rare, 1-in-6" sourcing lands it just above a Tier 1 weapon/armor, which reads right for a versatile craftable material).
  - `rules/core-rules.md` and `rules/character-creation.md`'s pointers updated to split correctly — `rules/equipment.md` for design, `rules/items.md` for the catalog.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding reference material, no `combatsimulations/` changes.

- **A1/A2 — two things flagged last entry both resolved directly by Drew, same session.** "luminova leaves can be ground into a powder to increase potency. they count as 2 different items that both deserve to exist" and "the storm seat is an official seat."
  - **Luminova Leaves/Powder**: not a duplication after all — a real crafting relationship, raw leaf (weaker, immediate) ground into powder (roughly double potency, needs processing). Gave Leaves the Source line it never had (harvestable in the field, Turnroot Weald's Luminova Clearing — same source as the Powder, just the unprocessed form) and a price (~15 gold, half the Powder's ~30, matching the roughly-halved healing). Added reciprocal cross-references on both entries (`items/hollow-and-weald-items.md`, `items/turnroot-weald-items.md`) and fixed `rules/items.md`'s catalog to describe the real relationship instead of flagging it as an unresolved overlap.
  - **The Storm Seat**: confirmed real, not a gap. Added a full entry to `mythology/seats.md` (matching the exact format already used for the other unbeared Seats — Death, Knowledge, Blood: a Domain line, no Bearer line, bearer status "not publicly known") and to `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` (added to the "known Seats include" list, plus a matching Known Archons-section entry). Both cite the Lightning Loop as the one confirmed Storm-aligned Artifact, carrying only a narrow slice (lightning) of the full domain. `items/lightning-loop.md`'s own open question about whether the Seat has a bearer stays genuinely open — Drew confirmed the Seat's existence, not who (if anyone) holds it — annotated rather than removed, so the file doesn't quietly overclaim more than was actually confirmed.
  - Not sim-related — pure worldbuilding, no `combatsimulations/` changes.

- **A3 — Design Principles, Exemplars, and the Alignment Checker restored from `archives/` to living status, Drew's direct call: "the design principles and exemplars we want back for sure. there still looks like there is value in alignment checker."** The 2026-07-15 doctrine-layer trim archived these with its own built-in revisit condition ("we can go back and check with fresh eyes after doing real creative work on tales untold for awhile") — that condition is now met and the answer came back: they should have stayed. Restored via `git mv` (history preserved) to `agent-tools/`, their original home.
  - **Restoration wasn't just a move — every stale cross-reference found and fixed**: `CLAUDE.md`'s Four Kinds section (item 3 rewritten from "archived, no longer maintained" to living-doctrine-again, with the full restore story), `CLAUDE.md`'s Agent Workflow step 2 (was "`red-team.md` is the one surviving review tool"), `agent-tools/README.md` (three new table rows + the header note), `agent-tools/red-team.md` (two `archives/design-principles.md` pointers), `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` (the exemplars pointer), `rules/invariants.md` (its scoping line). Also fixed inside the restored files themselves: `exemplars.md`'s Weck entry still pointed at `the-man-who-buys-wounds.md` (renamed to `-injuries` in the Wound→Injury sweep while the file sat archived) — a concrete example of why archived-but-referenced docs rot.
  - **One real content addition on restore**: the A3-adopted principle from 2026-07-18 ("A mechanic without impact doesn't matter. Meaning without mechanics doesn't either") moved into `design-principles.md` — its own memory.md adoption entry explicitly said it lived in memory *only because* no living doctrine file existed; the condition reversed, so the principle moved to its proper home, with a pointer back to the full adoption trail.
  - The generator layer (card-set-generator, encounter-generator, npc-encounter-generator) stays archived — Drew asked what else looks useful, answered in chat; nothing else restored without his say.

- **A2/A3 — player-perspective and card-compression restored, inspiration-guide folded rather than restored, per Drew's "bring em back" plus two specific asks.**
  - **`agent-tools/player-perspective.md`** restored via git mv AND refined against what the live playthrough actually surfaced — Drew's own question ("what if anything did my playthrough surface that could help refine the player-perspective.md?") answered in the file itself, not just in chat. Added one line to the roleplay prompt (react with INTENT in fiction terms — "I want to look at the top of the rubble pile," never "I take the Dig action") and a new **Lessons from the First Real Playthrough** section, six checks each earned by a real table moment: players declare curiosity not actions (the Rasp/rubble-pile miss); felt danger vs. mechanical danger are separate questions (the flat Borrower Settlement scene and its staging fix); smart-path discoverability (the scout-then-clear shortcut); environment-as-message (the water trail followed upstream); NPCs need bounded knowledge because players ask unscripted questions (Masaharu's "any leads?" vs. the farmer's unwritten knowledge); good roleplay expects a discount not a skip (the DC 5–7 rule).
  - **`agent-tools/card-compression.md`** restored unchanged — it was already sound; nothing this session's work would refine in it.
  - **`inspiration-guide.md` folded, not restored** — Drew: "can we fold in the useful bits of inspiration-guide.md that soul pass doesn't yet cover?" The real division found: the Soul Pass *detects* flatness (four evaluate questions, minimal-change constraints); the inspiration guide was the *generative* half — what to do once something comes back flat. Added as a new **When the Soul Pass Fails — Finding the Angle** section in `agent-tools/alignment-checker.md`: the six core questions (own logic, before/after, beautiful/horrifying overlap, consistent-rule-as-law, the child's mythic naming, what it wants on its own terms), the three condensed stuck-on lists (location/creature/NPC), and the last check ("could this exist in any TTRPG?"). Original stays in `archives/` untouched as the source snapshot, per convention — the fold is a copy-forward of the useful bits, not a move.
  - `agent-tools/README.md` table updated with all three. The generator layer (card-set/encounter/npc-encounter generators, codex-starter, translation-principle-full, the content snapshots) remains archived — nothing else restored.

- **A2/A3 — two new perspective tools (starter versions, explicitly meant to be refined through use) and a new root-level `unresolved-concerns.md`, all Drew's direct asks.**
  - **`agent-tools/mechanics-perspective.md`** — the systems-designer lens, deliberately distinct from red-team (which *attacks* finished content; this *evaluates* design quality — something can survive every attack and still be overpriced or inert). Built around what this session actually taught: action economy as the first lens (the Protect pricing lesson written in as the standing example), the three pillars, keyword honesty ("what is it doing BESIDES granting the keyword"), table cost, and comparable pricing against the nearest existing thing.
  - **`agent-tools/story-writer-perspective.md`** — the narrative-craft lens, deeper than the Soul Pass (tone fit) on story *construction*: theme nameable in one sentence (Sour Tomatoes as the standard), stakes that exist without the party, motive from the inside (Corvel as the example), what's withheld (the scar and the name; reasoning-leak as defect per CLAUDE.md's Do Not list), earned emotion, voice. Both marked as starters — "improve it when it misses something; that's the plan, not a failure."
  - **`unresolved-concerns.md`** (root, next to memory.md) — Drew: "so often I find these types of issues in memory.md or in standing reasoning. sound useful?" Yes, with one design constraint applied hard: it's an INDEX, not a third home — one or two lines per entry plus a pointer to the full trail, added when flagged, deleted when resolved (memory.md keeps history; the file only shows the present). Explicitly excludes deliberately-open questions (the repo's "open questions are on purpose" aesthetic is design, not debt) and Pending Propagation (operational staleness stays in memory.md where Sync looks for it). Seeded with 14 real open items swept from this file's own prose: Masaharu's Red overcount, Immunity's Positive-Status-Effects exclusion, DOUBLE DOWN's sim exclusion, the PvP lethality question, Initiative Shift possibly underpowered, the wheel rework, the Patient Host's undertuned kit + missing item progression, the unbalanced Greater Stonecoil, combat-example.md's stale pedagogy, Fog Goggles' missing Seat, the Bazaar's zero items + pricing-logic question, the Carrion Feather's Washed-Ashore trigger, the Lily/Moth/Hess-and-Cob candidates, and the glossary's dated keyword counts. `CLAUDE.md`'s Orient step now names it alongside memory.md.
  - Also in this exchange, for the record: Drew asked directly whether anything stops me from speaking openly or surfacing concerns. Answered honestly in chat — no, nothing structural; the workflow names Concerns as a step and he's rewarded pushback all session. The real miscalibration this session ran the opposite way (hedging on Luminova, already logged in Standing Reasoning).

- **A1/A2 — a full pass through the concerns ledger, one turn after building it: three more character files, two real fixes, and three items reclassified or corrected rather than left as written.**
  - **`characters/lily.md`, `characters/moth.md`, `characters/hess-and-cob.md`** — same split pattern as Kess/Corvel, each already duplicated across location files. Hess and Cob went in as ONE file, not two, deliberately: their entire content is the distance between them closing (she has the answer, he's the one whose questions are pointed at it) — matches Drew's own "&" framing of the request, and matches the black-maggie precedent for content whose real subject is a relationship, not two individuals. A real, standing inconsistency caught and fixed while building it: `locations/canille.md`'s own "Key Locations" section described the same person as "the oldest living scientist... Name pending" — written before Hess got named in the Key Characters section just below it, never reconciled. Fixed directly.
  - **Masaharu's Red overcount, actually fixed, not just flagged again.** Cut ENDURE (generic Resist/heal filler) from the Red core list, kept GROUNDING STANCE and BURN BRIGHT (both reinforce the immovable-Backline-sniper identity directly). Deck now correctly totals 5 Blue/3 Red/4 Green = 12, matching CTR12. Not in any ROSTER deck, so no sim regression risk.
  - **Immunity added to Positive Status Effects** (`rules/card-glossary.md`) per Drew's direct call — genuinely coherent addition, since it means a "remove all Positive Status Effects" card can now strip an unused Immunity charge before it triggers. Found a real follow-up while checking: the sim's shared status-transfer/strip code (`_transfer_statuses`/`_strip_one_status`, used by WAITING GAME/DRAIN/UNMAKE/LEVEL THE FIELD) still only knows Deadly/Resist/Evade/Protect — whether those specific cards should also touch Immunity is a real design call, not just a mechanical gap, so it went to `unresolved-concerns.md` rather than being silently wired in.
  - **Two ledger entries were miscategorized, corrected on Drew's direct read**: Fog Goggles' missing Seat-alignment and the Underground Bazaar's pricing-logic question are both open design (the repo's own "open questions are on purpose" register), not debt — removed the "reconcile"/"needs resolving" framing from both the ledger and `rules/items.md` itself. The Bazaar's *actual* debt (zero mechanical items exist at all) stayed, correctly separated from the pricing question that surrounds it.
  - **The PvP lethality entry was stale, citing a mechanism already superseded.** It described the old flat-25%-chance targeting as if still current; the team-targeting rework (random pick, pile on, drop on collapse) had already landed and dropped death-occurred rate from 45–62% to 41–48%. Corrected to reflect the actual current state — real progress, still short of target, still open pending real monster-vs-party test decks.
  - **The Patient Host entry clarified, not just repeated** — Drew's own genuine question ("I thought it was super strong?") answered directly: it wins decisively (84–91% vs. baseline), and that's not in question. What's flagged is a narrower, independent claim — whether its own signature *kit* (control/Initiative-Shift-flavored) matches what a Mind 8/Body 6/Soul 10 stat line would suggest, separate from whether the raw CTR gap (24 vs. 9) is doing most of the winning on its own. Split into two explicit threads in the ledger so the "is it strong" question and the "is the kit tuned right independent of stats" question don't read as one contradiction.
  - Not sim-related except the Masaharu fix (canon-only, no ROSTER entry, verified no regression risk).

- **A1 — keyword counts actually recounted, not estimated forward.** 241 card blocks across `cards/*.md` (up from 216 at the 2026-07-18 snapshot), every one of 28 glossary keywords rechecked case-insensitively rather than assumed to have grown proportionally. Real, uneven drift, worth noting: Deadly 11→19 and Ward 9→15 grew fast (the global dice-bump session leaned on both); Protect held at 4 (was counted as Fortress before the rename, same cards); Scry actually dropped 25→24 (a card lost its Scry somewhere in a rework, not investigated further); Reveal Hand grew from the named "(Break, Read)" pair to a real third card, FEINT, caught by checking the actual mechanic text rather than trusting the old attribution. Positive Status Effects (the term itself, not a keyword within it) jumped 1→5 cards now naming it directly. Vulnerable and Locked both confirmed still genuinely 0 — untouched by any card yet.

**[2026-07-23 6:06 AM CDT] The Patient Host's REGISTERED reworked — the actual tuning fix, not a full kit rewrite.** Mine to redesign per Drew's own direct confirmation of its origin.
